d. Capture SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and verify address = base{plus}3*stride.
. Pick the fault beat and its response (SLVERR or DECERR) from the scenario RNG and arm the responder to return that response once at the beat's address.
. Read the series back from one preload:
a. Load SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL with op=0b01 (Read), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
b. Shift four words carrying the beats' increment flags, then one trailing word (increment 0), through SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS; every Update-DR launches a read, and shift k returns the word and status bit of read k-1.
c. Verify the status bit is 1 only on the shift after the fault beat and every non-faulting beat returns the last word written to its address.
d. Capture SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and verify address = base{plus}3*stride.
. Issue a legal SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x1C) read of a preloaded slot outside the stream; verify OKAY and the word, proving recovery.
. Negative validation: `DTP_J2A_STATUS_BIT_NEGATIVE=1` (cocotb) or `+DTP_J2A_STATUS_BIT_NEGATIVE` (SV-UVM) keeps the expectation but leaves the fault unarmed, so the fault beat's checks must fail the run.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_otp_series_status_bit_chk` |`CHK-J2A-STATUS-BIT`: status bit 0 on every write-leg shift and 1 only on the read-leg shift after the fault beat
|`smc_otp_series_status_rdata_chk` |Each non-faulting read beat returns the last word written to its address
|`smc_otp_series_status_addr_chk` |SERIES_CTRL address readback equals base {plus} 3*stride after each leg (the held beat did not advance the address)
|`smc_otp_series_status_recover_chk` |A legal single read outside the stream returns its word with OKAY; the armed response reaches the bus and is consumed (`CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED` cocotb, `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ` SV-UVM)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The write leg completes clean, the read leg's fault surfaces on the following shift's status bit and nowhere else, every other beat returns its word, both SERIES_CTRL readbacks show base{plus}3*stride, and the recovery read completes OKAY, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-311-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_read_random_ops_test]]
===== 3.11 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_read_random_ops_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_rd_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC OTP randomized reads

*Test Coverage*:

* Constrained-random SMC OTP AXI4-Lite read addresses against a randomized 32-bit reference
* SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x1C) field packing under randomized address stimulus
* Per-op data integrity across a randomized address space
* Status correctness (OKAY) for all in-range randomized reads

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SMC OTP AXI-Lite subordinate with a randomized reference image over the read window.
. Generate a constrained-random sequence of in-range 32-bit read addresses.
. Issue each randomized read through the SMC OTP AXI-Lite bridge.
a. Load SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x1C); shift in the random address, data=0, wstrb=0, op=0b01 (Read); Update-DR.
b. Poll op status until not Busy (0b11); capture returned data and status.
. Compare each returned word with the AXI-Lite subordinate reference model at that address.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_otp_random_status_chk` |Every randomized read reaches op=0b00 (OKAY) after Busy clears
|`smc_otp_random_rdata_chk` |Each returned word matches the randomized AXI-Lite reference
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All randomized reads complete OKAY and every returned word matches the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-312-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_read_security_gating_test]]
===== 3.12 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_read_security_gating_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_rd_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC OTP read security gating

*Test Coverage*:

* `dbg_disable_i.smc_otp_jtag2axi = 1` gates the SMC OTP read bridge
* Gated read requests are suppressed: the TDR remains selected while no AXI-Lite traffic appears on `axil_smc_otp_jtag_req_o`, with no delayed replay after the disable is released
* Normal read operation restored after the disable clears, without any reset

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish a baseline SMC OTP read returning OKAY with expected data from the AXI-Lite subordinate.
. Gate (per pass, twice): assert `dbg_disable_i.smc_otp_jtag2axi = 1`, settle four toggling TCK cycles, load SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x1C), and shift a read request.
a. Verify no AXI-Lite transaction issues on `axil_smc_otp_jtag_req_o` across the gated window.
. Clear the disable without reset; verify no queued request replays, then verify normal reads resume.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_otp_rd_gate_noaxi_chk` |No AXI-Lite transaction on `axil_smc_otp_jtag_req_o` while gated
|`smc_otp_rd_gate_noreplay_chk` |No queued request replays after the disable's release
|`smc_otp_rd_gate_restore_chk` |Normal read with OKAY status resumes after the disable clears
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The asserted `dbg_disable_i.smc_otp_jtag2axi` suppresses all AXI-Lite activity with no delayed replay after release, and reads resume correctly once the disable clears, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-313-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_single_write_read_test]]
===== 3.13 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_single_write_read_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_rd_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP write-read path

*Test Coverage*:

* SEP OTP AXI4-Lite single-operation write followed by a read-back of the same address
* SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) DR field packing: 32-bit address, 32-bit data, wstrb, op
* CAPS reporting bus_type = AXI4-Lite, addr_size = 32, data_size = 32
* Data integrity between the written word and the value returned by the AXI-Lite subordinate

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SEP_OTP_JTAG2AXI_CAPS (IR=0x21) and read the 14-bit CAPS TDR.
a. Verify bus_type = AXI4-Lite (CAPS bit 0 = 1), addr_size = 32, data_size = 32.
. Issue a single write through the SEP OTP AXI-Lite bridge to a chosen 32-bit address.
a. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22).
b. Shift in target address, write data, wstrb=all-ones, op=0b10 (Write); Update-DR.
c. Poll until op status leaves Busy (0b11); verify op=0b00 (OKAY).
. Read back the same address through the SEP OTP AXI-Lite bridge.
a. Reload SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22).
b. Shift in target address, data=0, wstrb=0, op=0b01 (Read); Update-DR.
c. Poll op status until not Busy; read returned data and status from the DR.
. Compare the returned read word against the written value and the AXI-Lite subordinate model.
. Verify the terminal op status is 0b00 (OKAY) on both the write and the read.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_single_wr_rd_status_chk` |Write and read both reach op=0b00 (OKAY) after leaving Busy
|`sep_otp_single_wr_rd_rdata_chk` |Returned read word equals the written value and the AXI-Lite model
|`sep_otp_single_wr_rd_mem_chk` |AXI-Lite subordinate content at the target address matches the issued write
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The write completes OKAY, the read-back returns the same word with OKAY status, and the memory side effect matches, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-314-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_series_write_read_incr_test]]
===== 3.14 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_series_write_read_incr_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_rd_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP incrementing series write-read

*Test Coverage*:

* Sequential AXI4-Lite accesses through the SEP OTP bridge across a block of incrementing 32-bit addresses
* SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) preloading the start address and SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_INCR (IR=0x24) carrying each beat, the bridge advancing the series address by the beat size after every write
* Per-beat read data integrity against the data words written through the bridge
* OKAY terminal status in the SERIES_CTRL readback across the full series with no stuck-Busy condition
* Single-op addressing across many addresses is covered by <<sec-217-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_random_ops_test,2.17>> and <<sec-317-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_read_random_ops_test,3.17>>; every SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) shift carries its own address, so reissuing it over a contiguous block is not a distinct bridge property

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Choose a beat-aligned base address, a stride equal to the 32-bit beat, and a beat count N; draw a distinct data word per beat.
. Write the series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge.
a. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) with op=0b10 (Write), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
b. For each beat i in 0..N-1: shift the i-th data word through SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_INCR (IR=0x24); Update-DR issues the write at base{plus}i*stride and advances the series address.
c. After each Update-DR, wait for the AXI-Lite write handshake.
. Read the series back in the same incrementing order.
a. For each beat i: load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL with op=0b01 (Read), size, and address base{plus}i*stride; Update-DR.
b. Shift SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_INCR once to launch the read and wait for the AXI-Lite read handshake; shift it again and take the Capture-DR payload as the returned beat.
. Compare every returned beat with the data word written to that address.
. Capture SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and verify status = 0b00 (OKAY).

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_series_incr_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL readback reports OKAY (0b00) after the write and read series
|`sep_otp_series_incr_rdata_chk` |Each returned beat matches the data word written to its address
|`sep_otp_series_incr_mem_chk` |AXI-Lite subordinate content over the block matches the issued write series
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every incrementing-address write and read completes, the SERIES_CTRL readback reports OKAY, and all per-beat comparisons match the written data, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-315-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_series_write_read_no_incr_test]]
===== 3.15 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_series_write_read_no_incr_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_rd_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP fixed-address series write-read

*Test Coverage*:

* Repeated AXI4-Lite writes and reads through the SEP OTP bridge targeting one fixed 32-bit address (no address advance)
* SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) holding one address while SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x25) carries every beat without advancing it
* Returned data stability when the last-written value is read multiple times
* Series-length robustness with no DR/state corruption between repeated beats, and an unchanged address in the SERIES_CTRL readback

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Choose a single beat-aligned target address and draw N distinct data words.
. Write the N words to the fixed address through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge.
a. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) with op=0b10 (Write), size, and the target address; Update-DR.
b. For each word: shift it through SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x25); Update-DR issues the write and leaves the series address unchanged.
c. After each Update-DR, wait for the AXI-Lite write handshake.
. Read the fixed address N times through the bridge.
a. For each iteration: load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL with op=0b01 (Read), size, and the target address; Update-DR.
b. Shift SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR once to launch the read and wait for the AXI-Lite read handshake; shift it again and take the Capture-DR payload as the returned beat.
. Verify every returned beat equals the last word written.
. Capture SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and verify status = 0b00 (OKAY) and address = the target address.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_series_no_incr_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL readback reports OKAY (0b00) at the unchanged address after the series
|`sep_otp_series_no_incr_rdata_chk` |Every returned beat equals the last word written
|`sep_otp_series_no_incr_mem_chk` |AXI-Lite subordinate at the fixed address holds the last word written across the series
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All fixed-address beats complete, every returned beat equals the last word written, and the SERIES_CTRL readback reports OKAY at the unchanged address, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-316-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_series_write_read_incr_with_error_test]]
===== 3.16 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_series_write_read_incr_with_error_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_rd_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP write-read with error status

*Test Coverage*:

* AXI4-Lite write then read series through the SEP OTP bridge carried by SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x26), the series-data form whose shift selects the address increment per beat and returns the previous beat's error status
* The increment pattern 1, 0, 1, 1 on both legs: the second beat re-writes the held address, so the series spans three addresses and the later word wins at the held one
* A clean write leg with status bit 0 on every shift, then a read leg from one SERIES_CTRL preload in which one beat, chosen at random among the beats that first touch their address, returns SLVERR or DECERR
* Read-side Capture-DR returning the previous read's word and status bit: the bit is 1 only on the shift after the faulting read, every other beat returns the last word written to its address, and a held-address re-read after a faulting first read returns the word
* SERIES_CTRL readback reporting the pattern's final address after each leg, then a legal SINGLE_OP read outside the stream completing OKAY
* The error-series entries <<sec-425-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_write_with_status_test,4.25>> and <<sec-426-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_read_with_status_test,4.26>> cover the same series-data form on incrementing-only streams together with the SERIES_CTRL fault code

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Choose a beat-aligned base address whose four-slot footprint fits the target window and draw a data word per beat of the increment pattern 1, 0, 1, 1.
. Write the series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge.
a. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) with op=0b10 (Write), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
b. For each beat: shift the data word and the beat's increment flag through SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x26); Update-DR issues the write and advances the series address only when the flag is set.
c. After each Update-DR, wait for the AXI-Lite write handshake, verify the returned status bit is 0, and confirm the AXI-Lite subordinate holds the data word at the beat's address; shift one trailing word (data 0, increment 0) and verify its status bit is 0.
d. Capture SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and verify address = base{plus}3*stride.
. Pick the fault beat and its response (SLVERR or DECERR) from the scenario RNG and arm the responder to return that response once at the beat's address.
. Read the series back from one preload:
a. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL with op=0b01 (Read), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
b. Shift four words carrying the beats' increment flags, then one trailing word (increment 0), through SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS; every Update-DR launches a read, and shift k returns the word and status bit of read k-1.
c. Verify the status bit is 1 only on the shift after the fault beat and every non-faulting beat returns the last word written to its address.
d. Capture SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and verify address = base{plus}3*stride.
. Issue a legal SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) read of a preloaded slot outside the stream; verify OKAY and the word, proving recovery.
. Negative validation: `DTP_J2A_STATUS_BIT_NEGATIVE=1` (cocotb) or `+DTP_J2A_STATUS_BIT_NEGATIVE` (SV-UVM) keeps the expectation but leaves the fault unarmed, so the fault beat's checks must fail the run.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_series_status_bit_chk` |`CHK-J2A-STATUS-BIT`: status bit 0 on every write-leg shift and 1 only on the read-leg shift after the fault beat
|`sep_otp_series_status_rdata_chk` |Each non-faulting read beat returns the last word written to its address
|`sep_otp_series_status_addr_chk` |SERIES_CTRL address readback equals base {plus} 3*stride after each leg (the held beat did not advance the address)
|`sep_otp_series_status_recover_chk` |A legal single read outside the stream returns its word with OKAY; the armed response reaches the bus and is consumed (`CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED` cocotb, `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ` SV-UVM)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The write leg completes clean, the read leg's fault surfaces on the following shift's status bit and nowhere else, every other beat returns its word, both SERIES_CTRL readbacks show base{plus}3*stride, and the recovery read completes OKAY, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-317-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_read_random_ops_test]]
===== 3.17 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_read_random_ops_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_rd_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP randomized reads

*Test Coverage*:

* Constrained-random SEP OTP AXI4-Lite read addresses against a randomized 32-bit reference
* SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) field packing under randomized address stimulus
* Per-op data integrity across a randomized address space
* Status correctness (OKAY) for all in-range randomized reads

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SEP OTP AXI-Lite subordinate with a randomized reference image over the read window.
. Generate a constrained-random sequence of in-range 32-bit read addresses.
. Issue each randomized read through the SEP OTP AXI-Lite bridge.
a. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22); shift in the random address, data=0, wstrb=0, op=0b01 (Read); Update-DR.
b. Poll op status until not Busy (0b11); capture returned data and status.
. Compare each returned word with the AXI-Lite subordinate reference model at that address.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_random_status_chk` |Every randomized read reaches op=0b00 (OKAY) after Busy clears
|`sep_otp_random_rdata_chk` |Each returned word matches the randomized AXI-Lite reference
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All randomized reads complete OKAY and every returned word matches the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-318-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_read_security_gating_test]]
===== 3.18 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_read_security_gating_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_rd_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP read security gating

*Test Coverage*:

* `dbg_disable_i.sep_otp_jtag2axi = 1` gates the SEP OTP read bridge
* Gated read requests are suppressed: the TDR remains selected while no AXI-Lite traffic appears on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o`, with no delayed replay after the disable is released
* Normal read operation restored after the disable clears, without any reset

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish a baseline SEP OTP read returning OKAY with expected data from the AXI-Lite subordinate.
. Gate (per pass, twice): assert `dbg_disable_i.sep_otp_jtag2axi = 1`, settle four toggling TCK cycles, load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22), and shift a read request.
a. Verify no AXI-Lite transaction issues on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o` across the gated window.
. Clear the disable without reset; verify no queued request replays, then verify normal reads resume.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_rd_gate_noaxi_chk` |No AXI-Lite transaction on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o` while gated
|`sep_otp_rd_gate_noreplay_chk` |No queued request replays after the disable's release
|`sep_otp_rd_gate_restore_chk` |Normal read with OKAY status resumes after the disable clears
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The asserted `dbg_disable_i.sep_otp_jtag2axi` suppresses all AXI-Lite activity with no delayed replay after release, and reads resume correctly once the disable clears, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-4-jtag2axi-error-backpressure-cdc-and-decode-scenarios]]
==== 4. JTAG2AXI Error, Backpressure, CDC, and Decode Scenarios

[[sec-41-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_single_write_test]]
===== 4.1 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_single_write_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric single write error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op write through the SMC fabric AXI4 debug bridge (SMC_AXI_SINGLE_OP, IR=0x28) targeting an error-generating region
* AXI4 subordinate returns a non-OKAY write response (BRESP = SLVERR or DECERR) captured in op[1:0] (0b10 / 0b01)
* No spurious or partial write side effect lands at the targeted error address on `axi_smc_dbg_req_o`
* Bridge does not hang or corrupt internal state; a subsequent legal write completes with OKAY

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4 subordinate (OcahAxiSlaveAgent) to return SLVERR or DECERR on writes to the chosen error region.
. Load SMC_AXI_SINGLE_OP via `OcahJtagMasterDriver` (IR=0x28):
a. Shift in the error-region target address, write data, wstrb = all-ones, size, op = 0b10 (Write).
b. Update-DR to launch the AXI4 transaction on `axi_smc_dbg_req_o`.
. Poll the op-status field via Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy (op != 0b11).
. Verify op[1:0] reflects the injected error code (0b01 SLVERR or 0b10 DECERR) and not OKAY (0b00).
. Confirm no committed write at the error address in the AXI subordinate model.
. Issue a single-op write to a known-good address; verify OKAY and correct address/data/wstrb at the subordinate, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_axi_err_single_wr_status_chk` |Op-status reports the injected SLVERR/DECERR write-response code
|`smc_axi_err_single_wr_mem_chk` |No unintended write side effect at the error-region address
|`smc_axi_err_single_wr_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY (no hang/corruption)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The single write error is reported in the op-status, no spurious memory update occurs, and the bridge recovers for the next legal transaction, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-42-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_single_read_test]]
===== 4.2 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_single_read_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric single read error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op read through the SMC fabric AXI4 debug bridge (IR=0x28) targeting an error-generating region
* AXI4 subordinate returns a non-OKAY read response (RRESP = SLVERR or DECERR) captured in op[1:0] (0b01 / 0b10)
* Read-data field is ignored or held at the bridge-defined value when the response is non-OKAY
* Bridge recovers: a following legal read returns correct data with OKAY

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4 subordinate to return SLVERR or DECERR on reads to the error region.
. Load SMC_AXI_SINGLE_OP via `OcahJtagMasterDriver` (IR=0x28):
a. Shift in the error-region target address, data = 0, wstrb = 0, size, op = 0b01 (Read).
b. Update-DR to launch the AXI4 read on `axi_smc_dbg_req_o`.
. Poll the op-status via Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy.
. Verify op[1:0] reflects the injected error code and that the read-data field is treated as invalid (ignored / defined value).
. Issue a single-op read to a known-good address holding a reference value; verify OKAY and matching read data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_axi_err_single_rd_status_chk` |Op-status reports the injected SLVERR/DECERR read-response code
|`smc_axi_err_single_rd_rdata_chk` |Returned read data is ignored/held to the defined value on error
|`smc_axi_err_single_rd_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The single read error is reported in the op-status, the read-data field is handled per spec, and the bridge recovers for the next legal read, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-43-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_no_incr_write_test]]
===== 4.3 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_no_incr_write_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric no-increment write error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI fixed-address (no-incr) write series through the SMC fabric AXI4 bridge, carried by SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) and SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x2B)
* Mid-series SLVERR/DECERR injected on one beat; address mode remains fixed for every beat
* SERIES_CTRL status identifies the failing beat while good beats commit correctly to the fixed address
* Bridge recovers: the series can be re-run or a new legal access completes with OKAY

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate to return a non-OKAY response on a selected beat at the fixed target address.
. Load SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) with op=0b10 (Write), size, and the fixed address; Update-DR.
. Shift each beat through SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x2B); every Update-DR issues a write at the fixed address.
. After each beat's AXI write handshake, check the memory at the fixed address; after the faulting beat, capture SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code, good beats committed their data to the fixed address, the faulting beat left the memory unchanged, and the AXI address never incremented on `axi_smc_dbg_req_o`.
. Issue a follow-up legal SMC_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x28) write to a clean address; verify OKAY, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_axi_err_noincr_wr_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL status captured after the failing beat carries the injected error code
|`smc_axi_err_noincr_wr_mem_chk` |Good beats commit to the fixed address; no address increment observed
|`smc_axi_err_noincr_wr_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The injected fault is reported on the failing beat, the address stays fixed for all beats, good beats commit correctly, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-44-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_no_incr_read_test]]
===== 4.4 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_no_incr_read_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric no-increment read error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI fixed-address (no-incr) read series through the SMC fabric AXI4 bridge, carried by SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) and SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x2B)
* Mid-series SLVERR/DECERR injected on one beat while the AXI address remains fixed
* Good beats return the reference value at the fixed address; failing beat marks invalid data
* SERIES_CTRL status matches the injected fault; bridge recovers afterward

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate with a known value at the fixed address and a non-OKAY response on a selected read beat.
. For each beat: load SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) with op=0b01 (Read), size, and the fixed address; Update-DR.
. Shift SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x2B) once to launch the read and wait for the AXI read handshake; shift it again to capture the returned beat.
. After the faulting beat, capture SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify good beats return the reference data, the SERIES_CTRL status after the failing beat carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code with that beat's read data treated as invalid, and the address never incremented.
. Issue a follow-up legal SMC_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x28) read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_axi_err_noincr_rd_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL status captured after the failing read beat carries the injected error code
|`smc_axi_err_noincr_rd_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the fixed-address reference; failing beat data is invalid
|`smc_axi_err_noincr_rd_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Good beats match the fixed-address reference, the failing beat reports the injected error, the address stays fixed, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-45-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_incr_write_test]]
===== 4.5 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_incr_write_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric incrementing write error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address write series through the SMC fabric AXI4 bridge, carried by SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) and SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_INCR (IR=0x2A)
* One beat in the auto-incrementing sequence hits an error region and returns SLVERR/DECERR
* Good beats before/after the fault commit to their correctly-incremented addresses
* SERIES_CTRL status identifies the failing beat; bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate to return a non-OKAY response at one address within the incrementing range.
. Load SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) with op=0b10 (Write), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
. Shift each beat through SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_INCR (IR=0x2A); every Update-DR issues a write and advances the series address by the stride.
. After each beat's AXI write handshake, check the memory at the beat's address; after the faulting beat, capture SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify good beats committed at their incremented addresses, the faulting beat left its address unchanged, the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code, and the address progression on `axi_smc_dbg_req_o` matches the expected increment.
. Issue a follow-up legal SMC_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x28) write to a clean address; verify OKAY, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_axi_err_incr_wr_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL status captured after the failing beat carries the injected error code
|`smc_axi_err_incr_wr_mem_chk` |Good beats commit at correctly-incremented addresses
|`smc_axi_err_incr_wr_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Good beats commit at the expected incrementing addresses, the failing beat reports the injected error, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-46-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_incr_read_test]]
===== 4.6 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_incr_read_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric incrementing read error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address read series through the SMC fabric AXI4 bridge, carried by SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) and SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_INCR (IR=0x2A)
* One address in the auto-incrementing range returns SLVERR/DECERR mid-series
* Good beats before the fault return reference data at their incremented addresses
* SERIES_CTRL status matches the injected fault; bridge recovers afterward

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Pre-load the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate with reference data across the address range and configure a non-OKAY response at one address.
. For each beat i: load SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) with op=0b01 (Read), size, and address base{plus}i*stride; Update-DR.
. Shift SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_INCR (IR=0x2A) once to launch the read and wait for the AXI read handshake; shift it again to capture the returned beat.
. After the faulting beat, capture SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify good beats return the reference data at their incremented addresses, the SERIES_CTRL status after the failing beat carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code with that beat's read data treated as invalid, and the address progression matches the expected increment.
. Issue a follow-up legal SMC_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x28) read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_axi_err_incr_rd_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL status captured after the failing read beat carries the injected error code
|`smc_axi_err_incr_rd_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the incremented-address reference data
|`smc_axi_err_incr_rd_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Good beats match the expected incrementing-address references, the failing beat reports the injected error, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-47-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_incr_write_with_status_test]]
===== 4.7 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_incr_write_with_status_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric incrementing write with error-status TDR

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address write series through the SMC fabric AXI4 bridge, carried by SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) and SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x2C), with a fault mid-series
* Each with-error-status shift returns the previous beat's error status, so the fault surfaces on the shift after the faulting beat without a SERIES_CTRL capture per beat
* A trailing shift returns the last beat's status; SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
* Good beats commit at their incremented addresses and the faulting beat leaves its address unchanged; bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate to return a non-OKAY response at one address within the incrementing range.
. Load SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) with op=0b10 (Write), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
. Shift each beat with increment 1 through SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x2C) and run the series to completion; each Capture-DR returns the status bit of the previous beat.
. After the last beat, shift one trailing word (data 0, increment 0) to return the last beat's status bit; after the faulting beat, capture SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify the status bit is 1 only on the shift after the faulting beat, good beats committed at their incremented addresses, the faulting beat left its address unchanged, and the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code.
. Issue a follow-up legal SMC_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x28) write to a clean address; verify OKAY, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_axi_err_incr_wr_status_tdr_chk` |The status bit is 1 on the shift after the faulting beat and 0 on every other shift; SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
|`smc_axi_err_incr_wr_status_mem_chk` |Good beats commit at correctly-incremented addresses
|`smc_axi_err_incr_wr_status_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The with-error-status bit and the SERIES_CTRL status report the failing beat and error code, good beats commit, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-48-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_incr_read_with_status_test]]
===== 4.8 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_series_incr_read_with_status_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric incrementing read with error-status TDR

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address read series through the SMC fabric AXI4 bridge, carried by SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) and SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x2C), with a fault mid-series
* One SERIES_CTRL preload serves the whole read stream: the bridge advances the address per launching shift, and each shift returns the data and error status of the previous read
* Good beats return reference data with status bit 0; the shift returning the faulting read carries status bit 1 and SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
* Bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Pre-load the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate with reference data and configure a non-OKAY response at one address in the incrementing range.
. Load SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x29) with op=0b01 (Read), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
. Shift N words with increment 1 followed by one trailing word with increment 0 through SMC_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x2C); shift k returns the data and status bit of read k-1.
. Capture SMC_AXI_SERIES_CTRL right after the faulting read's handshake and read its status field.
. Verify good beats returned the reference data at their incremented addresses, the status bit is 1 only for the faulting read, and the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code.
. Issue a follow-up legal SMC_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x28) read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_axi_err_incr_rd_status_tdr_chk` |The status bit is 1 only on the shift returning the faulting read; SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
|`smc_axi_err_incr_rd_status_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the incremented-address reference data
|`smc_axi_err_incr_rd_status_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The with-error-status bit and the SERIES_CTRL status report the failing read beat and error code, good beats match references, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-49-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_security_gating_test]]
===== 4.9 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_security_gating_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric error-path security gating

*Test Coverage*:

* SMC fabric bridge gating: `dbg_disable_i.smc_jtag2axi = 1`
* When gated, SMC_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x28) requests are suppressed: no AXI traffic on `axi_smc_dbg_req_o`, even on error-path attempts, and no error-status update
* Two assert/release passes prove the gate is repeatable; each release restores normal and error-path operation

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. With the subordinate configured to return errors on the target region, assert `dbg_disable_i.smc_jtag2axi = 1` to gate the bridge.
. Load SMC_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x28) and attempt an error-region write:
a. Verify no AXI transaction is issued on `axi_smc_dbg_req_o` and no error-status latches.
. Clear the disable without reset; verify a normal (and error-path) JTAG2AXI access resumes and the error is reported as expected.
. Repeat steps 1–3 as a second pass of the same disable.
. Confirm that with all disables clear, ungated error-path accesses complete and report SLVERR/DECERR normally.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_axi_err_gating_no_traffic_chk` |No AXI traffic on `axi_smc_dbg_req_o` while gated
|`smc_axi_err_gating_restore_chk` |Each release restores normal/error-path operation
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The asserted disable suppresses all AXI traffic on the error path, and operation is fully restored after each release, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-410-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_single_write_test]]
===== 4.10 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_single_write_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC OTP single write error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op write through the SMC OTP AXI4-Lite debug bridge (SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP, IR=0x1C) targeting an error region
* AXI4-Lite subordinate returns a non-OKAY write response (SLVERR/DECERR) captured in op[1:0]
* No spurious write side effect lands at the targeted error address on `axil_smc_otp_jtag_req_o`
* Bridge recovers: a subsequent legal write completes with OKAY

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate (AXI-Lite wrapper) to return SLVERR/DECERR on writes to the error region.
. Load SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP via `OcahJtagMasterDriver` (IR=0x1C):
a. Shift in the 32-bit error-region address, write data, wstrb = all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write).
b. Update-DR to launch the AXI4-Lite transaction on `axil_smc_otp_jtag_req_o`.
. Poll the op-status via Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy.
. Verify op[1:0] reflects the injected error code (0b01 SLVERR or 0b10 DECERR), not OKAY.
. Confirm no committed write at the error address in the subordinate model.
. Issue a single-op write to a known-good address; verify OKAY and correct address/data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_otp_axi_err_single_wr_status_chk` |Op-status reports the injected SLVERR/DECERR write-response code
|`smc_otp_axi_err_single_wr_mem_chk` |No unintended write side effect at the error-region address
|`smc_otp_axi_err_single_wr_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY (no hang/corruption)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The single write error is reported in the op-status, no spurious update occurs on the AXI-Lite subordinate, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-411-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_single_read_test]]
===== 4.11 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_single_read_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC OTP single read error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op read through the SMC OTP AXI4-Lite bridge (IR=0x1C) targeting an error region
* AXI4-Lite subordinate returns a non-OKAY read response (SLVERR/DECERR) captured in op[1:0]
* Read-data field is ignored or held at the bridge-defined value on a non-OKAY response
* Bridge recovers: a following legal read returns correct data with OKAY

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate to return SLVERR/DECERR on reads to the error region.
. Load SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP via `OcahJtagMasterDriver` (IR=0x1C):
a. Shift in the 32-bit error-region address, data = 0, wstrb = 0, op = 0b01 (Read).
b. Update-DR to launch the AXI4-Lite read on `axil_smc_otp_jtag_req_o`.
. Poll the op-status until not Busy.
. Verify op[1:0] reflects the injected error code and the read-data field is treated as invalid.
. Issue a single-op read to a known-good address holding a reference value; verify OKAY and matching data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_otp_axi_err_single_rd_status_chk` |Op-status reports the injected SLVERR/DECERR read-response code
|`smc_otp_axi_err_single_rd_rdata_chk` |Returned read data is ignored/held to the defined value on error
|`smc_otp_axi_err_single_rd_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The single read error is reported in the op-status, the read-data field is handled per spec, and the bridge recovers for the next legal read, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

===== 4.14 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_series_no_incr_write_test`

* JTAG2AXI fixed-address (no-incr) write series through the SMC OTP AXI4-Lite bridge, carried by SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x1D) and SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x1F)
* Mid-series SLVERR/DECERR injected on one beat; AXI-Lite address remains fixed for every beat
* SERIES_CTRL status identifies the failing beat while good beats commit correctly to the fixed address
* Bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate to return a non-OKAY response on a selected beat at the fixed target address.
. Load SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x1D) with op=0b10 (Write), size, and the fixed address; Update-DR.
. Shift each beat through SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x1F); every Update-DR issues a write at the fixed address.

. Verify the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code, good beats committed their data to the fixed address, the faulting beat left the memory unchanged, and the AXI-Lite address never incremented on `axil_smc_otp_jtag_req_o`.

|===

'''''

[[sec-415-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_series_no_incr_read_test]]
===== 4.15 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_series_no_incr_read_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC OTP no-increment read error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI fixed-address (no-incr) read series through the SMC OTP AXI4-Lite bridge, carried by SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x1D) and SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x1F)
* Mid-series SLVERR/DECERR injected on one beat while the AXI-Lite address remains fixed
* Good beats return the reference value at the fixed address; failing beat marks invalid data
* SERIES_CTRL status matches the injected fault; bridge recovers afterward

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate with a known value at the fixed address and a non-OKAY response on a selected read beat.
. For each beat: load SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x1D) with op=0b01 (Read), size, and the fixed address; Update-DR.
. Shift SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x1F) once to launch the read and wait for the AXI-Lite read handshake; shift it again to capture the returned beat.
. After the faulting beat, capture SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify good beats return the reference data, the SERIES_CTRL status after the failing beat carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code with that beat's read data treated as invalid, and the address never incremented.
. Issue a follow-up legal SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x1C) read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_otp_axi_err_noincr_rd_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL status captured after the failing read beat carries the injected error code
|`smc_otp_axi_err_noincr_rd_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the fixed-address reference; failing beat data is invalid
|`smc_otp_axi_err_noincr_rd_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Good beats match the fixed-address reference, the failing beat reports the injected error, the address stays fixed, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-416-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_series_incr_write_with_status_test]]
===== 4.16 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_series_incr_write_with_status_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC OTP write with error-status TDR

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address write series through the SMC OTP AXI4-Lite bridge, carried by SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x1D) and SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x20), with a fault mid-series
* Each with-error-status shift returns the previous beat's error status, so the fault surfaces on the shift after the faulting beat without a SERIES_CTRL capture per beat
* A trailing shift returns the last beat's status; SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
* Good beats commit at their incremented addresses and the faulting beat leaves its address unchanged; bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate to return a non-OKAY response at one address within the incrementing range.
. Load SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x1D) with op=0b10 (Write), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
. Shift each beat with increment 1 through SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x20) and run the series to completion; each Capture-DR returns the status bit of the previous beat.
. After the last beat, shift one trailing word (data 0, increment 0) to return the last beat's status bit; after the faulting beat, capture SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify the status bit is 1 only on the shift after the faulting beat, good beats committed at their incremented addresses, the faulting beat left its address unchanged, and the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code.
. Issue a follow-up legal SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x1C) write to a clean address; verify OKAY, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_status_tdr_chk` |The status bit is 1 on the shift after the faulting beat and 0 on every other shift; SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
|`smc_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_status_mem_chk` |Good beats commit at correctly-incremented addresses
|`smc_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_status_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The with-error-status bit and the SERIES_CTRL status report the failing beat and error code, good beats commit, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-417-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_series_incr_read_with_status_test]]
===== 4.17 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_series_incr_read_with_status_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC OTP read with error-status TDR

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address read series through the SMC OTP AXI4-Lite bridge, carried by SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x1D) and SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x20), with a fault mid-series
* One SERIES_CTRL preload serves the whole read stream: the bridge advances the address per launching shift, and each shift returns the data and error status of the previous read
* Good beats return reference data with status bit 0; the shift returning the faulting read carries status bit 1 and SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
* Bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Pre-load the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate with reference data and configure a non-OKAY response at one address in the incrementing range.
. Load SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x1D) with op=0b01 (Read), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
. Shift N words with increment 1 followed by one trailing word with increment 0 through SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x20); shift k returns the data and status bit of read k-1.
. Capture SMC_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL right after the faulting read's handshake and read its status field.
. Verify good beats returned the reference data at their incremented addresses, the status bit is 1 only for the faulting read, and the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code.
. Issue a follow-up legal SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x1C) read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_tdr_chk` |The status bit is 1 only on the shift returning the faulting read; SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
|`smc_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the incremented-address reference data
|`smc_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The with-error-status bit and the SERIES_CTRL status report the failing read beat and error code, good beats match references, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-418-dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_security_gating_test]]
===== 4.18 `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_error_security_gating_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SMC OTP error-path security gating

*Test Coverage*:

* SMC OTP bridge gating: `dbg_disable_i.smc_otp_jtag2axi = 1`
* When gated, SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x1C) requests are suppressed: no AXI-Lite traffic on `axil_smc_otp_jtag_req_o`, even on error-path attempts, and no error-status update
* Two assert/release passes prove the gate is repeatable; each release restores normal and error-path operation

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. With the AXI-Lite subordinate configured to return errors on the target region, assert `dbg_disable_i.smc_otp_jtag2axi = 1` to gate the bridge.
. Load SMC_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x1C) and attempt an error-region write:
a. Verify no AXI-Lite transaction is issued on `axil_smc_otp_jtag_req_o` and no error-status latches.
. Clear the disable without reset; verify normal/error-path operation resumes and the error is reported as expected.
. Repeat steps 1–3 as a second pass of the same disable.
. Confirm that with all disables clear, ungated error-path accesses complete and report SLVERR/DECERR normally.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`smc_otp_axi_err_gating_no_traffic_chk` |No AXI-Lite traffic on `axil_smc_otp_jtag_req_o` while gated
|`smc_otp_axi_err_gating_restore_chk` |Each release restores normal/error-path operation
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The asserted disable suppresses all AXI-Lite traffic on the error path, and operation is fully restored after each release, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-419-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_single_write_test]]
===== 4.19 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_single_write_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP single write error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op write through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite debug bridge (SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP, IR=0x22) targeting an error region
* AXI4-Lite subordinate returns a non-OKAY write response (SLVERR/DECERR) captured in op[1:0]
* No spurious write side effect lands at the targeted error address on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o`
* Bridge recovers: a subsequent legal write completes with OKAY

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate to return SLVERR/DECERR on writes to the error region.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP via `OcahJtagMasterDriver` (IR=0x22):
a. Shift in the 32-bit error-region address, write data, wstrb = all-ones, op = 0b10 (Write).
b. Update-DR to launch the AXI4-Lite transaction on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o`.
. Poll the op-status via Capture-DR/Shift-DR until not Busy.
. Verify op[1:0] reflects the injected error code (0b01 SLVERR or 0b10 DECERR), not OKAY.
. Confirm no committed write at the error address in the subordinate model.
. Issue a single-op write to a known-good address; verify OKAY and correct address/data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_single_wr_status_chk` |Op-status reports the injected SLVERR/DECERR write-response code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_single_wr_mem_chk` |No unintended write side effect at the error-region address
|`sep_otp_axi_err_single_wr_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY (no hang/corruption)
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The single write error is reported in the op-status, no spurious update occurs on the AXI-Lite subordinate, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-420-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_single_read_test]]
===== 4.20 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_single_read_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP single read error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op read through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge (IR=0x22) targeting an error region
* AXI4-Lite subordinate returns a non-OKAY read response (SLVERR/DECERR) captured in op[1:0]
* Read-data field is ignored or held at the bridge-defined value on a non-OKAY response
* Bridge recovers: a following legal read returns correct data with OKAY

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate to return SLVERR/DECERR on reads to the error region.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP via `OcahJtagMasterDriver` (IR=0x22):
a. Shift in the 32-bit error-region address, data = 0, wstrb = 0, op = 0b01 (Read).
b. Update-DR to launch the AXI4-Lite read on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o`.
. Poll the op-status until not Busy.
. Verify op[1:0] reflects the injected error code and the read-data field is treated as invalid.
. Issue a single-op read to a known-good address holding a reference value; verify OKAY and matching data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_single_rd_status_chk` |Op-status reports the injected SLVERR/DECERR read-response code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_single_rd_rdata_chk` |Returned read data is ignored/held to the defined value on error
|`sep_otp_axi_err_single_rd_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The single read error is reported in the op-status, the read-data field is handled per spec, and the bridge recovers for the next legal read, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-421-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_write_test]]

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_err_test`

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address write series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge, carried by SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) and SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_INCR (IR=0x24)
* One beat in the auto-incrementing sequence returns SLVERR/DECERR
* Good beats before/after the fault commit to their correctly-incremented addresses
* SERIES_CTRL status identifies the failing beat; bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate to return a non-OKAY response at one address within the incrementing range.

. After each beat's AXI-Lite write handshake, check the memory at the beat's address; after the faulting beat, capture SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify good beats committed at their incremented addresses, the faulting beat left its address unchanged, the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code, and the address progression on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o` matches the expected increment.
. Issue a follow-up legal SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) write to a clean address; verify OKAY, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL status captured after the failing beat carries the injected error code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_mem_chk` |Good beats commit at correctly-incremented addresses
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Good beats commit at the expected incrementing addresses, the failing beat reports the injected error, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

. Issue a follow-up legal SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL status captured after the failing read beat carries the injected error code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the incremented-address reference data
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Good beats match the expected incrementing-address references, the failing beat reports the injected error, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

* Mid-series SLVERR/DECERR injected on one beat; AXI-Lite address remains fixed for every beat

|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_wr_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The injected fault is reported on the failing beat, the address stays fixed, good beats commit correctly, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_status_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The with-error-status bit and the SERIES_CTRL status report the failing beat and error code, good beats commit, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-426-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_read_with_status_test]]
===== 4.26 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_read_with_status_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP read with error-status TDR

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address read series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge, carried by SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) and SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x26), with a fault mid-series
* One SERIES_CTRL preload serves the whole read stream: the bridge advances the address per launching shift, and each shift returns the data and error status of the previous read
* Good beats return reference data with status bit 0; the shift returning the faulting read carries status bit 1 and SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
* Bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Pre-load the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate with reference data and configure a non-OKAY response at one address in the incrementing range.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) with op=0b01 (Read), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
. Shift N words with increment 1 followed by one trailing word with increment 0 through SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x26); shift k returns the data and status bit of read k-1.
. Capture SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL right after the faulting read's handshake and read its status field.
. Verify good beats returned the reference data at their incremented addresses, the status bit is 1 only for the faulting read, and the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code.
. Issue a follow-up legal SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_tdr_chk` |The status bit is 1 only on the shift returning the faulting read; SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the incremented-address reference data
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The with-error-status bit and the SERIES_CTRL status report the failing read beat and error code, good beats match references, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-427-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_security_gating_test]]
===== 4.27 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_security_gating_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP error-path security gating

*Test Coverage*:

* SEP OTP bridge gating: `dbg_disable_i.sep_otp_jtag2axi = 1`
* When gated, SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) requests are suppressed: no AXI-Lite traffic on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o`, even on error-path attempts, and no error-status update
* Two assert/release passes prove the gate is repeatable; each release restores normal and error-path operation

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. With the AXI-Lite subordinate configured to return errors on the target region, assert `dbg_disable_i.sep_otp_jtag2axi = 1` to gate the bridge.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) and attempt an error-region write:
a. Verify no AXI-Lite transaction is issued on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o` and no error-status latches.
. Clear the disable without reset; verify normal/error-path operation resumes and the error is reported as expected.
. Repeat steps 1–3 as a second pass of the same disable.
. Confirm that with all disables clear, ungated error-path accesses complete and report SLVERR/DECERR normally.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_gating_no_traffic_chk` |No AXI-Lite traffic on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o` while gated
|`sep_otp_axi_err_gating_restore_chk` |Each release restores normal/error-path operation
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Any required enable low forces BYPASS with no AXI-Lite traffic on the error path, and operation is fully restored after each enable is restored, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-428-dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_aw_before_w_test]]
===== 4.28 `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_aw_before_w_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: AW before W channel skew

*Test Coverage*:

* Maximum AW/W channel skew on the JTAG2AXI single-op write path with AW ready asserted while W ready is held low
* Reachable `AXI_SEND_ADDR_W -++>++ AXI_WAIT_BRESP` FSM transition on `axi_state_q_tclk`
* AXI-Lite `aw_valid_stable` / `w_valid_stable` and payload-stability assertions while ready is low
* `AXI_SEND_DATA_W` is structurally unreachable: AW{plus}W are pushed atomically through the per-channel `axi_cdc_clearable` FIFOs, so the FSM history skips it

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive a JTAG2AXI single-op write through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`:
a. Program the bridge address and data via the primary TAP and launch the write op.
b. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate to skew the channels: accept AW (ready high) while holding W ready low for an extended interval.
. Observe the bridge FSM and channels under skew:
a. Sample the FSM history mirror and confirm the sequence `AXI_IDLE -++>++ AXI_SEND_ADDR_W -++>++ AXI_WAIT_BRESP -++>++ AXI_UPDATE_STATUS -++>++ AXI_IDLE`.
b. Confirm `axi_state_q_tclk == AXI_SEND_DATA_W` is never observed at any sample point.
c. While W ready is low, confirm AW/W valid and payload remain stable and do not deassert.
. Release the W-channel backpressure and let the write drain.
. Verify the write completes with an OKAY response and read back the written location to confirm the value landed.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_bp_fsm_chk` |FSM traverses `AXI_SEND_ADDR_W -++>++ AXI_WAIT_BRESP` and never enters `AXI_SEND_DATA_W`
|`j2a_bp_stable_chk` |AW/W valid and payload remain stable while the subordinate holds ready low
|`j2a_bp_status_chk` |Final JTAG2AXI completion status is OKAY and read-back data matches the written value
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The reachable maximum-skew path and AXI-Lite stable assertions are exercised, and the write completes correctly with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-429-dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_long_stall_test]]
===== 4.29 `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_long_stall_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Long ready stalls

*Test Coverage*:

* Long subordinate ready stalls on AW, W, AR, and R channels exercised one channel at a time and combined
* Real success hits on the AXI-Lite `aw_valid_stable` / `w_valid_stable` / `ar_valid_stable` and corresponding payload `+*_stable+` assertions
* Reachable JTAG2AXI FSM dwell in `AXI_SEND_ADDR_W` / `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` (write) and the read-issue / wait states under prolonged stall
* No premature timeout, abort, or status corruption while a legal stall is in progress

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Set up stall stimulus on the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate:
a. Configure long, deterministic ready-low intervals on AW/W (write path) and AR/R (read path) that remain inside the architectural stall budget.
. Issue write traffic through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`:
a. Launch a single-op write while AW/W ready are stalled; observe valid/payload hold stable across the full stall window.
b. Release the stall and confirm OKAY completion.
. Issue read traffic through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`:
a. Launch a single-op read while AR ready (then R ready) is stalled; observe AR valid/address hold stable.
b. Release the stall and confirm the returned data matches the reference model.
. Verify the bridge does not declare a timeout or error during any legal stall and that each operation reaches its expected terminal status after release.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_long_stall_assert_chk` |AW/W/AR valid and payload `+*_stable+` assertions hold across the full stall window
|`j2a_long_stall_status_chk` |No premature timeout/abort; each op reaches its expected terminal status after stall release
|`j2a_long_stall_data_chk` |Post-stall write-back and read-data compare against the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All AW/W/AR stable assertions receive real success hits under long stalls, no operation times out or aborts during a legal stall, and every read/write completes correctly after release with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-430-dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_abort_at_data_w_test]]
===== 4.30 `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_abort_at_data_w_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Reset abort around data phase

*Test Coverage*:

* A system reset (`rst_n_i`, the bridges' AXI-side reset) asserted while a SINGLE_OP write is held on the W channel of each bridge by a responder READY stall that is released only after the reset
* The bridge observed mid-flight before the reset through `dtp_tb_if`: its TCK-domain AXI FSM outside `AXI_IDLE` with the single-op pending flag set (the CDC accepts AW and W at once, so the FSM waits in `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` while the responder holds the channel)
* Containment: the FSM back in `AXI_IDLE` within a bound after the reset, the CDC's TCK-side isolate-and-clear observed, and no write for the aborted operation reaching the subordinate
* Recovery: the SINGLE_OP status leaving `BUSY_OR_FULL` within eight polls and a legal write completing afterwards
* A bridge that stays in `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` with status `BUSY_OR_FULL` after the reset fails `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM` and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY`; the scenario is the regression for that defect

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each bridge: hold the responder's W channel with a READY stall that outlasts the scenario, record the target slot's prior word, and issue a SINGLE_OP write through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`.
. Step TCK in Run-Test/Idle while sampling the bridge FSM and pending flag through `dtp_tb_if` until the FSM leaves `AXI_IDLE`, bounded in TCK cycles (the FSM and the CDC's TCK side advance only while TCK runs); record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT`.
. Clear the sticky CDC clear-seen flag, pulse `rst_n_i` for one to three system cycles, then release the stall.
. Step TCK while sampling the FSM until it returns to `AXI_IDLE`, bounded in TCK cycles; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`. Record `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` from the sticky clear-seen flag and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` from the unchanged target slot.
. Poll the SINGLE_OP status up to eight times; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` against `SUCCESS`, and on success issue a legal write to a clean slot with the memory compare.
. After all three bridges, fail the pass naming the bridges whose status stayed `BUSY_OR_FULL`.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT` |The FSM has left `AXI_IDLE` and the pending flag is set when the reset lands
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM` |The FSM is back in `AXI_IDLE` within the bound after the reset
|`CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` |The bridge's CDC ran its TCK-side isolate-and-clear after the reset
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` |The aborted operation's slot in the subordinate memory is unchanged
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` |The SINGLE_OP status leaves `BUSY_OR_FULL` within eight polls and the follow-on legal write completes
|===

*Pass Criteria*: On every bridge the reset lands with the FSM observed mid-flight, the FSM returns to `AXI_IDLE`, the CDC clear is observed, no write escapes, and the status recovers so the follow-on write completes, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-431-dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_narrow_reset_mid_xaction_test]]
===== 4.31 `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_narrow_reset_mid_xaction_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Narrow reset during transaction

*Test Coverage*:

* A system reset (`rst_n_i`, the bridges' AXI-side reset) asserted while a SINGLE_OP write is held on the AW channel of each bridge by a responder READY stall that is released only after the reset
* The bridge observed mid-flight before the reset through `dtp_tb_if`: its TCK-domain AXI FSM outside `AXI_IDLE` with the single-op pending flag set (the CDC accepts AW and W at once, so the FSM waits in `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` while the responder holds the channel)
* Containment: the FSM back in `AXI_IDLE` within a bound after the reset, the CDC's TCK-side isolate-and-clear observed, and no write for the aborted operation reaching the subordinate
* Recovery: the SINGLE_OP status leaving `BUSY_OR_FULL` within eight polls and a legal write completing afterwards
* The single-cycle pulse exercises the `axi_cdc_clearable` reset controllers' isolate-and-clear on the TCK side; the sticky clear-seen flag in `dtp_tb_if` is the observation
* A bridge that stays in `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` with status `BUSY_OR_FULL` after the reset fails `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM` and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY`; the scenario is the regression for that defect

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each bridge: hold the responder's AW channel with a READY stall that outlasts the scenario, record the target slot's prior word, and issue a SINGLE_OP write through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`.
. Step TCK in Run-Test/Idle while sampling the bridge FSM and pending flag through `dtp_tb_if` until the FSM leaves `AXI_IDLE`, bounded in TCK cycles (the FSM and the CDC's TCK side advance only while TCK runs); record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT`.
. Clear the sticky CDC clear-seen flag, pulse `rst_n_i` for a single system cycle, then release the stall.
. Step TCK while sampling the FSM until it returns to `AXI_IDLE`, bounded in TCK cycles; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`. Record `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` from the sticky clear-seen flag and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` from the unchanged target slot.
. Poll the SINGLE_OP status up to eight times; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` against `SUCCESS`, and on success issue a legal write to a clean slot with the memory compare.
. After all three bridges, fail the pass naming the bridges whose status stayed `BUSY_OR_FULL`.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT` |The FSM has left `AXI_IDLE` and the pending flag is set when the reset lands
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM` |The FSM is back in `AXI_IDLE` within the bound after the reset
|`CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` |The bridge's CDC ran its TCK-side isolate-and-clear after the reset
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` |The aborted operation's slot in the subordinate memory is unchanged
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` |The SINGLE_OP status leaves `BUSY_OR_FULL` within eight polls and the follow-on legal write completes
|===

*Pass Criteria*: On every bridge the reset lands with the FSM observed mid-flight, the FSM returns to `AXI_IDLE`, the CDC clear is observed, no write escapes, and the status recovers so the follow-on write completes, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-432-dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_back_to_back_reset_test]]
===== 4.32 `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_back_to_back_reset_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Back-to-back CDC reset recovery

*Test Coverage*:

* CDC reset re-entry with back-to-back reset pulses across the TCK-to-ACLK domains
* `cdc_reset_ctrlr_half` IDLE / ISOLATE / CLEAR recovery-path coverage including re-entry while a prior clear is still settling
* All bridge FIFOs and FSMs converge to a clean idle after repeated resets (no deadlock or stuck handshake)
* Post-recovery reads and writes complete correctly across all involved clock domains

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start active bridge traffic through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`.
. Apply back-to-back CDC resets:
a. Issue a second reset pulse before the first clear/isolate sequence has fully settled, forcing CDC reset re-entry.
b. Sample the CDC controller and confirm it cycles through the IDLE / ISOLATE / CLEAR recovery states and does not deadlock on re-entry.
. Verify all bridge FIFOs and FSMs settle to a clean idle after the reset burst.
. Issue subsequent read and write operations and confirm each reaches its expected terminal status with correct data.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_state_chk` |CDC controller exercises IDLE/ISOLATE/CLEAR recovery paths through reset re-entry without deadlock
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_settle_chk` |All bridge FIFOs/FSMs return to a clean idle after the back-to-back reset burst
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_recovery_chk` |Post-recovery reads/writes complete correctly with expected status and data
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Back-to-back CDC resets exercise the IDLE/ISOLATE/CLEAR recovery paths and reset re-entry without deadlock, all bridge state settles cleanly, and subsequent operations complete correctly with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-433-dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_write_test]]
===== 4.33 `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_write_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: CTN default-slave write DECERR

*Test Coverage*:

* CTN AXI-Lite xbar default-slave write DECERR path triggered by a write to an unmapped address
* Xbar `+default_*_mst_port[_en]+` write-decode branch and default-slave response generation
* No mapped CTN register receives a side effect from the unmapped write
* Bridge recovers and accepts a subsequent legal mapped write

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Issue an unmapped write:
a. Drive a JTAG2AXI write to an address with no valid CTN xbar slave so it routes to the default DECERR slave.
. Verify the error response and monitor:
a. Confirm the write completion reports DECERR.
b. Confirm the OCAH-local XTRIG monitor observes the decode miss / default-slave routing event.
. Verify no side effects:
a. Compare all mapped CTN register/memory contents against the reference model and confirm none changed.
. Issue a follow-on legal mapped write and confirm it completes with OKAY and the expected value lands.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_decerr_wr_status_chk` |Unmapped write reports DECERR via the default-slave path
|`j2a_decerr_wr_mem_chk` |No mapped CTN register/memory side effect from the unmapped write
|`j2a_decerr_wr_recovery_chk` |A subsequent legal mapped write completes with OKAY
|===
