[[sec-617-dtp_ctm_p2p_cla_to_cla_test]]
===== 6.17 `dtp_ctm_p2p_cla_to_cla_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_p2p_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: P2P CLA to CLA

*Test Coverage*:

* CTM point-to-point routing entirely between internal CLA channels (single source CLA to single destination CLA)
* `+CT_SRC[cla_dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` programmed with exactly one source-input bit set
* Request appears only at the selected destination CLA; no spurious triggers elsewhere
* No feedback loop where the source CLA re-triggers itself

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program the intra-CLA point-to-point route via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write `+CT_SRC[cla_dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT = (1 << src)+` so the destination CLA output selects the single source input.
b. Read the register back and confirm only the one source-input bit is set.
. Drive the source CLA request via the OCAH-local CTM BFM.
. Observe the CLA destinations with the OCAH-local CTM monitor:
a. Verify the request reaches exactly the selected destination CLA.
b. Verify no other CLA channel observes the request and the source is not fed back to itself.
. Repeat for several source/destination CLA combinations.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_p2p_cla_to_cla_csr_chk` |CSR readback of the destination CLA output register shows exactly one source-input bit set
|`ctm_p2p_cla_to_cla_route_chk` |Trigger reaches exactly the selected CLA destination against the reference model
|`ctm_p2p_cla_to_cla_nospur_chk` |No spurious triggers and no source self-retrigger
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The CLA-to-CLA trigger is delivered only to the configured destination with no spurious activity or feedback, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-618-dtp_ctm_p2p_ctp_to_ctp_test]]
===== 6.18 `dtp_ctm_p2p_ctp_to_ctp_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_p2p_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: P2P CTP to CTP

*Test Coverage*:

* CTM point-to-point routing entirely between external CTP ports (single source CTP to single destination CTP)
* `+CT_SRC[ctp_dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` programmed with exactly one source-input bit set
* Request appears only at the selected destination CTP; no spurious triggers elsewhere
* No feedback loop from the destination CTP back into the source route

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program the inter-CTP point-to-point route via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write `+CT_SRC[ctp_dst]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT = (1 << src)+` so the destination CTP output selects the single source input.
b. Read the register back and confirm only the one source-input bit is set.
. Drive the source CTP request via the OCAH-local CTP BFM.
. Observe the CTP destinations with the OCAH-local CTP monitor:
a. Verify the request reaches exactly the selected destination CTP.
b. Verify no other CTP port observes the request and no feedback loop re-triggers the source.
. Repeat for several source/destination CTP combinations.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_p2p_ctp_to_ctp_csr_chk` |CSR readback of the destination CTP output register shows exactly one source-input bit set
|`ctm_p2p_ctp_to_ctp_route_chk` |Trigger reaches exactly the selected CTP destination against the reference model
|`ctm_p2p_ctp_to_ctp_nospur_chk` |No spurious triggers and no source feedback loop
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The CTP-to-CTP trigger is delivered only to the configured destination with no spurious activity or feedback, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-619-dtp_ctm_reset_wire_or_mode_test]]
===== 6.19 `dtp_ctm_reset_wire_or_mode_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: CTM reset in wire-OR mode

*Test Coverage*:

* System reset (`rst_n_i`) clears all CTM wire-OR routing registers to `0x0000_0000`
* No spurious cross-trigger requests on any destination during or after reset
* CTM accepts new wire-OR configuration after reset and routes correctly
* Pre-reset multicast routing and active trigger traffic do not survive the reset

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish pre-reset wire-OR state:
a. Program one or more `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` with multi-bit `CT_DST_SELECT` masks via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`.
b. Drive source pulses and confirm the multicast destinations respond.
. Assert system reset (`rst_n_i`) while wire-OR routes are active, then wait for reset deassertion.
. Verify clearing and quiescence:
a. Read all `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` and confirm each is `0x0000_0000`.
b. Confirm the OCAH-local CTM/CTP monitors observe no spurious trigger on any destination during or after reset.
. Reconfigure a fresh wire-OR route post-reset, pulse the source, and verify correct multicast routing resumes.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_reset_wire_or_mode_csr_chk` |All `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` read back `0x0000_0000` after reset
|`ctm_reset_wire_or_mode_nospur_chk` |No spurious trigger on any destination during or after reset
|`ctm_reset_wire_or_mode_route_chk` |Post-reset wire-OR reconfiguration routes correctly against the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Reset clears all wire-OR routing state with no spurious destination toggles, and post-reset multicast routing matches the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-620-dtp_ctm_reset_p2p_mode_test]]
===== 6.20 `dtp_ctm_reset_p2p_mode_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: CTM reset in P2P mode

*Test Coverage*:

* System reset (`rst_n_i`) clears all CTM point-to-point routing registers to `0x0000_0000`
* In-flight point-to-point handshake state is cleared by reset
* No spurious cross-trigger requests on any destination during or after reset
* A post-reset point-to-point transaction completes correctly

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish pre-reset P2P state:
a. Program one or more `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` with single-bit `CT_DST_SELECT` masks via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`.
b. Start a point-to-point handshake on a configured source.
. Assert system reset (`rst_n_i`) mid-handshake, then wait for deassertion.
. Verify clearing and quiescence:
a. Read all `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` and confirm each is `0x0000_0000`.
b. Confirm the in-flight handshake state is cleared and the monitors observe no spurious trigger during or after reset.
. Reprogram a single-bit P2P route post-reset, drive the source, and verify the transaction completes only at the selected destination.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_reset_p2p_mode_csr_chk` |All `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` read back `0x0000_0000` after reset
|`ctm_reset_p2p_mode_nospur_chk` |No spurious trigger and no stale handshake state after reset
|`ctm_reset_p2p_mode_route_chk` |Post-reset point-to-point transaction completes at the selected destination
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Reset clears all point-to-point routing and handshake state with no spurious activity, and a post-reset P2P transaction completes correctly, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-621-dtp_ctm_reset_all_modes_test]]
===== 6.21 `dtp_ctm_reset_all_modes_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: CTM reset across all modes

*Test Coverage*:

* System reset (`rst_n_i`) clears CTM routing state programmed across both wire-OR and point-to-point modes
* All `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` return to `0x0000_0000` default regardless of pre-reset mode mix
* No spurious cross-trigger requests on any destination during or after reset
* Both wire-OR and point-to-point routes can be reprogrammed and route correctly post-reset

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Establish a mixed pre-reset configuration:
a. Program some sources with multi-bit (wire-OR) `CT_DST_SELECT` masks and others with single-bit (P2P) masks via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`.
b. Drive source pulses across both modes and confirm correct routing before reset.
. Assert system reset (`rst_n_i`) while both route classes are active, then wait for deassertion.
. Verify clearing and quiescence:
a. Read all `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` and confirm each is `0x0000_0000`.
b. Confirm the monitors observe no spurious trigger on any destination during or after reset.
. Reprogram both a wire-OR and a point-to-point route post-reset, pulse the sources, and verify each routes correctly.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_reset_all_modes_csr_chk` |All `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` read back `0x0000_0000` after reset regardless of pre-reset mode
|`ctm_reset_all_modes_nospur_chk` |No spurious trigger on any destination during or after reset
|`ctm_reset_all_modes_route_chk` |Post-reset wire-OR and P2P reconfiguration both route correctly against the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Reset returns all routing state to defaults for both modes with no spurious activity, and both wire-OR and P2P routes reprogram correctly, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-622-dtp_ctm_rand_all_scenarios_test]]
===== 6.22 `dtp_ctm_rand_all_scenarios_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTM all scenarios

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized `CT_DST_SELECT` masks across all CTM sources spanning both wire-OR and P2P routing
* Random source/destination pairs and trigger timing across CLA and CTP classes
* All observed routing matches the CTM reference model for every random configuration
* No routing corruption or state leakage between successive random configurations
* Wire-OR superposition correct whenever random masks overlap on a destination

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations (e.g. 20):
a. Generate random `CT_DST_SELECT` values for all CTM sources, mixing single-bit and multi-bit masks across CLA and CTP destinations.
b. Write all `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` registers via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent` and read each back to confirm the programmed mask.
c. Drive random cross-trigger requests on random source ports via the OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs with randomized timing.
d. Verify each request appears at exactly its configured destinations and that overlapping destinations OR correctly.
e. Confirm no residual routing carries over from the previous iteration's configuration.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_all_scenarios_csr_chk` |Every randomized `CT_DST_SELECT` reads back as programmed
|`ctm_rand_all_scenarios_route_chk` |All observed routing matches the reference model across iterations
|`ctm_rand_all_scenarios_or_chk` |Overlapping random masks OR correctly at shared destinations
|`ctm_rand_all_scenarios_noleak_chk` |No routing state leaks between successive configurations
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every random mixed-mode configuration routes exactly as modeled with correct wire-OR superposition and no inter-configuration leakage, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-623-dtp_ctm_rand_wire_or_only_test]]
===== 6.23 `dtp_ctm_rand_wire_or_only_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTM wire-OR only

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized multi-bit `CT_DST_SELECT` masks restricted to wire-OR (multicast) routing only
* Random source pulses fan out to all selected destinations simultaneously
* Wire-OR superposition correct when random masks overlap on shared destinations
* Non-selected destinations remain quiet for every random configuration

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations:
a. Generate random `CT_DST_SELECT` values constrained to two or more set bits per active source (wire-OR-only).
b. Write all active `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` registers via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent` and read each back.
c. Drive random source pulses via the OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs.
d. Verify each pulse asserts every selected destination simultaneously and the routing mask is honored.
e. Where random masks overlap, verify the shared destination ORs the contributing sources; verify non-selected destinations stay idle.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_wire_or_only_csr_chk` |Every randomized multicast mask reads back as programmed
|`ctm_rand_wire_or_only_route_chk` |All selected destinations assert per the reference model across iterations
|`ctm_rand_wire_or_only_or_chk` |Overlapping random sources OR correctly at shared destinations
|`ctm_rand_wire_or_only_nospur_chk` |No non-selected destination toggles
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every random wire-OR configuration multicasts correctly with correct superposition and no spurious destination toggles, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-624-dtp_ctm_rand_p2p_only_test]]
===== 6.24 `dtp_ctm_rand_p2p_only_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTM P2P only

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized single-bit `CT_DST_SELECT` masks restricted to point-to-point routing only
* Random request/acknowledge handshakes complete at exactly the selected destination
* No cross-route leakage between independent point-to-point routes
* Coverage of diverse single-bit destination positions across sources

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations:
a. Generate random `CT_DST_SELECT` values constrained to exactly one set bit per active source (P2P-only).
b. Write all active `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` registers via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent` and read each back to confirm a single destination bit is set.
c. Drive randomized handshakes on random source ports via the OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs.
d. Verify each request/ack completes only at its configured single destination.
e. Verify no other route observes the request (no cross-route leakage).

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_p2p_only_csr_chk` |Every randomized P2P mask reads back with exactly one destination bit set
|`ctm_rand_p2p_only_route_chk` |Each request/ack completes at the selected destination per the reference model
|`ctm_rand_p2p_only_noleak_chk` |No cross-route leakage between independent point-to-point routes
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every random point-to-point configuration completes its handshake at exactly the configured destination with no cross-route leakage, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-625-dtp_ctm_rand_cla_to_ctp_test]]
===== 6.25 `dtp_ctm_rand_cla_to_ctp_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTM CLA to CTP

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized CTM routes restricted to internal CLA sources targeting external CTP destinations
* Random single-bit and multi-bit `CT_DST_SELECT` masks and random trigger timing within the CLA-to-CTP class
* Selected CTP destinations match the random configuration; non-selected stay quiet
* Coverage of diverse CLA-source x CTP-destination route combinations

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations:
a. Randomize `CT_DST_SELECT` masks for CLA sources, with destination bits restricted to CTP ports.
b. Write the active `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` registers via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent` and read each back.
c. Drive CLA triggers via the OCAH-local CTM BFM with randomized timing.
d. Observe CTP destinations via the OCAH-local CTP monitor and verify the selected CTP ports respond.
e. Sample the source x destination coverage of the exercised CLA-to-CTP combinations.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_cla_to_ctp_csr_chk` |Every randomized CLA-to-CTP mask reads back as programmed
|`ctm_rand_cla_to_ctp_route_chk` |Selected CTP destinations respond per the reference model across iterations
|`ctm_rand_cla_to_ctp_nospur_chk` |No non-selected CTP destination toggles
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every random CLA-to-CTP configuration routes correctly to its selected CTP destinations with no spurious activity and full route-combination coverage, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-626-dtp_ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_test]]
===== 6.26 `dtp_ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTM CTP to CLA

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized CTM routes restricted to external CTP sources targeting internal CLA destinations
* Random single-bit and multi-bit `CT_DST_SELECT` masks and random trigger timing within the CTP-to-CLA class
* Selected CLA destinations match the random configuration; non-selected stay quiet
* Coverage of diverse CTP-source x CLA-destination route combinations

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations:
a. Randomize `CT_DST_SELECT` masks for CTP sources, with destination bits restricted to CLA channels.
b. Write the active `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` registers via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent` and read each back.
c. Drive CTP triggers via the OCAH-local CTP BFM with randomized timing.
d. Observe CLA destinations via the OCAH-local CTM monitor and verify the selected CLA channels respond.
e. Sample the source x destination coverage of the exercised CTP-to-CLA combinations.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_csr_chk` |Every randomized CTP-to-CLA mask reads back as programmed
|`ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_route_chk` |Selected CLA destinations respond per the reference model across iterations
|`ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_nospur_chk` |No non-selected CLA destination toggles
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every random CTP-to-CLA configuration routes correctly to its selected CLA destinations with no spurious activity and full route-combination coverage, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-627-dtp_ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test]]
===== 6.27 `dtp_ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM BFM

*Objective*: CTM CSR deterministic sweep

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic closure of CTM generated-regblock byte-enable and masked-write condition holes that random selection can miss
* Full-word, per-byte, and masked writes to `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` with readback after each
* Reserved bits read back as zero and are unaffected by writes
* Internal route decode tracks each programmed `CT_DST_SELECT` value

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the XTRIG CSR path to idle and disable all CTM source routing.
. Sweep the CTM routing CSR fields deterministically via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Apply full-word writes, then per-byte-enable writes, then masked writes to `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` to exercise each generated byte-enable and masked-write condition.
b. Read back after each write and compare the defined destination-select field against the expected value.
c. Confirm reserved bits remain zero and are not disturbed by adjacent writes.
. For representative swept values, confirm the internal route decode matches the programmed `CT_DST_SELECT` via the OCAH-local CTM monitor.
. Disable all CTM source routing before test exit.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_csr_chk` |Full-word, byte-enable, and masked-write readbacks match expected `CT_DST_SELECT` values
|`ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_rsvd_chk` |Reserved bits read back zero and are undisturbed
|`ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_route_chk` |Internal route decode tracks the programmed destination mask against the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every byte-enable and masked-write condition in the CTM regblock is exercised and reads back correctly with reserved bits clear and route decode matching, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-628-dtp_ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_test]]
===== 6.28 `dtp_ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM BFM

*Objective*: CTM all-source select coverage

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic closure of per-source select mapping for every defined CTM source-select CSR (`CT_SRC[0].CONFIG_0` through `CT_SRC[25].CONFIG_0`)
* Single-destination, paired-destination, and inverted-destination `CT_DST_SELECT` masks written and read back at every source index
* No source index aliases another CTM source register
* Direct exercise of all generated CTM source-select mapping code in the default DTP configuration

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Assert power-on reset, bring the XTRIG CSR path to idle, and disable all CTM source routing.
. For every defined CTM source index, via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write a single-destination `CT_DST_SELECT` mask, read back, and compare the defined destination-select field.
b. Write a paired-destination mask, read back, and compare.
c. Write an inverted-destination mask, read back, and compare.
d. Confirm the written value affects only this source register and no neighboring `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` (no aliasing).
. Sample per-source select coverage so every `CT_SRC1..25_CONFIG_0` mapping is touched.
. Disable all CTM source routing again before test exit.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_csr_chk` |Every defined source-select CSR accepts and returns the single/paired/inverted mask
|`ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_alias_chk` |No source index aliases another CTM source register
|`ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_route_chk` |Per-source route decode matches the programmed mask against the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every defined CTM source-select CSR is touched with single, paired, and inverted masks and reads back correctly with no aliasing across source indices, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

SMC fabric write/read scenarios map to the source modules recorded in the
scenario tables.

[[dtp-formal-plan]]
=== Formal Verification Plan

Formal property checking corroborates the control-logic rows of the
traceability matrix; it does not replace the simulation credit. The open-path
flow runs SymbiYosys through the runner's formal mode
(`tools/dv/doc/run-dv.adoc`, Formal verification) and follows the property
style in `hw/common/dv/docs/formal-property-style.adoc`. `dtp_formal_cfg.toml`
and `testlists/formal.toml` are the launch entry; the properties, the bind
files, the task files and the reset environments live under `formal/`. The
plan names nine targets, T1 to T9, over three formal tops: `dtp` for the
JTAG-domain blocks, `jtag2axi` for the bridge control, and
`cross_trigger_network` for the single-clock cross-trigger logic. Every
target row names the traceability rows it corroborates, the app checks that
apply, the path that runs them, and the item `run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode
formal` launches.

[[dtp-formal-targets]]
==== Targets

A target is a described block whose behaviour is a boolean over a small
control state: a state machine, a handshake, a gating term, a register with a
fixed access policy. Data movement is out (<<dtp-formal-exclusions>>).

[cols="1,3,3,2,2",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Described block |Rows |Formal top and item |Path
|T1 TAP controller |`jtag_tap_ctrlr`: the sixteen-state Moore machine, the saturating TMS counter, the scan-control flags |F1.1, F1.2 |`dtp`, `dtp_tap_fpv` |Open
|T2 Instruction register and TDR selection |`jtag_inst_reg` (6-bit shift, 64-bit one-hot decode, IDCODE on reset) and the PTAP select and TDO multiplexer terms: bypass for undefined opcodes and for disabled features, one TDR selected per instruction, zero-length bypass, the read-only IDCODE and CAPS captures |F1.3, F1.4, F1.5 (selection), F2.5 |`dtp`, `dtp_ir_fpv` |Open
|T3 Reset hierarchy and persistence |`jtag_trst_n = trst_n AND pwr_on_rst_ni`, `rst_n = !test_logic_reset`, `chrst_n` under `jtag_tmp`; `jtag_tmp_status_reg`; `jtag_ic_reset_reg` with `reset_hold`; `jtag_3dcr_reg` with `config_hold` |F1.2, F1.6, F2.1, F2.2 |`dtp`, `dtp_reset_fpv` |Open
|T4 STAP selection and debug disable |The PTAP 3DCR select, the four `jtag_stap` instances (I/O, SMC, SEP, extra) with their 3DCR and lockup flops, the `stap_host` return path in `jtag_intf_unit`, the two-flop `dbg_disable_i` synchronizers |F3.3, F3.4, F3.5, F3.6 |`dtp`, `dtp_stap_fpv` |Open; STAP chain order and disable wiring also as licensed connectivity
|T5 iJTAG SIB network and debug disable |The three `prim_jtag_sib_mux_post` instances (DFT secure, DFT non-secure, DFD) |F3.1, F3.2, F3.6 |`dtp`, `dtp_sib_fpv` |Open
|T6 Debug control and clock stop |`jtag_debug_ctrl_reg` with its `cla_clock_stop` synchronizer; `ctn_clock_stop_ctrl` |F2.3, F2.4, F5.7 |`dtp`, `dtp_dbgctl_fpv`; `cross_trigger_network`, `ctn_clkstop_fpv` |Open
|T7 JTAG2AXI bridge control |`jtag2axi`: the seven-state request machine, the TCK-side admit counters, the ACLK outstanding counters, the status encoder, the disable gate and its flush |F4.3 (status encoding), F4.4 (control), F4.5 |`jtag2axi`, `jtag2axi_ctrl_fpv` |Open for the control with the AXI side free; the CDC transport and the mid-transaction reset as licensed X-propagation and reset checks
|T8 Cross-trigger port and matrix |`ctp_handshake_ctrl` (sender and receiver), `ctp_core` mode multiplexers and wire-OR edge detect, `ctp_pulse_stretcher`, one `ctm_src_selector` slice |F5.1, F5.2, F5.3, F5.6 |`cross_trigger_network`, `ctn_xtrig_fpv` |Open
|T9 Cross-trigger CSR access |The CTN address map into the AXI-Lite crossbar and its error subordinate; the generated register blocks' field access, strobes and reset values |F5.4, F5.5 |`cross_trigger_network`, `ctn_csr_fpv` |Open for the address-map contract and the field policy of one CTP and one CTM slice; the full register sweep derived from the SystemRDL as the licensed register app
|===

[[dtp-formal-exclusions]]
==== Exclusions

[cols="3,4",options="header",]
|===
|Logic |Reason and where the credit lives
|JTAG2AXI data path: the 132-bit shared shift register with a variable active length, the byte-lane shifts by `addr mod BEAT_BYTES`, the 56-bit series address increment and its wrap, the `DEADBEEF` pending-read capture |Wide arithmetic and per-bit loops; a bounded proof over them is a data check the F4.1 and F4.2 scenarios make with data verification. The bridge target proves control only.
|The five gray-coded clearable CDC FIFOs with three-stage synchronizers and the coupled reset controller inside `axi_cdc_clearable` |Third-party IP with its own correctness argument; the open path takes the AXI side as free inputs. A reset that lands mid-transaction leaves the request machine waiting with no recovery other than Test-Logic-Reset (the F4.4 gap); that path is a licensed reset and X-propagation check, not an open-path property.
|Boundary-scan data and the external iJTAG instrument chains |Loopbacks in `tb_top`; the design carries selection and gating only, which T2 and T5 cover.
|`ctp_pulse_stretcher` at large `STRETCH_MULT` |A 16-bit down counter, up to 65 536 cycles. End-to-end proofs assume `STRETCH_MULT <= 4`; the counter's reload and terminal behaviour is one inductive property of its own.
|The full 26 x 26 cross-trigger matrix |A generate of identical selector slices; one slice with a free select mask stands for the array.
|The AXI-Lite crossbar internals (demux address-write lock, per-port FIFOs) |Vendor IP; T9 checks the address-map contract at the CTN port and the register blocks behind it.
|The pad-ring wire-OR bus |The shared wire, pull-up and inversion contract live outside the RTL; a wire-OR property needs an environment model of the bus and is not a DUT property.
|TDO retiming and the lockup flops |Negedge flops are ordinary state under `multiclock on`; the timing margin they buy is not a property.
|Deriving `dbg_disable_t`, the SMU-composed IC_RESET external slice, real downstream TAPs |Integration level, as for the simulation bench.
|===

[[dtp-formal-properties]]
==== Properties by target

Every label follows the style chapter: `ast_` assertions, `asm_` assumptions,
`cov_` covers, each a boolean over current and `$past` values. Priority P0
marks the properties that need an unbounded proof (`prove`) before any
release; P1 and P2 sign off on a bounded result at the target's depth
(<<dtp-formal-depths>>). The P0 set is the security-gating set plus the reset
hierarchy: every disable field gates exactly its path, and no reset source
reaches a register the standard reserves for another.

T1 is the property module in `formal/props/dtp_tap_props.sv`, bound to
`jtag_tap_ctrlr` by `formal/props/dtp_tap_bind.sv`:

[cols="3,3,1,1",options="header",]
|===
|Label |Checks |Row |Prio
|`ast_tap_state_one_hot` |The state register holds exactly one state |F1.1 |P0
|`ast_tap_five_tms_high_reach_tlr` |A saturated TMS counter implies Test-Logic-Reset |F1.2 |P0
|`ast_tap_tlr_holds_on_tms_high`, `ast_tap_tlr_exits_to_rti`, `ast_tap_rti_holds_on_tms_low`, `ast_tap_shift_holds_on_tms_low`, `ast_tap_update_dr_only_from_exit`, `ast_tap_update_ir_only_from_exit` |The legal TMS arcs into and out of the reset, idle, shift and update states |F1.1 |P1
|`ast_tap_select_exclusive`, `ast_tap_moore_flags_follow_state`, `ast_tap_negedge_flags_follow_state` |The DR and IR selects are exclusive; the Moore flags and the negedge-clocked scan-control flags follow the state |F1.1 |P1
|===

The remaining targets, one property module per block under
`formal/props/`, with the intents the labels carry:

[cols="1,3,4,1",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Module |Property intents |Prio
|T2 |`dtp_ir_props.sv` on `jtag_inst_reg` and the PTAP select terms |`ast_ir_decoded_one_hot`: the decoded instruction is one-hot. `ast_ir_capture_loads_01`: Capture-IR loads `6'b000001`. `ast_ir_update_decodes_shift_reg`: after Update-IR the decoded instruction is `1 << shift_reg`. `ast_ir_reset_is_idcode`: Test-Logic-Reset, TRST and power-on reset leave IDCODE decoded. `ast_tdr_select_one_hot`: at most one TDR select is high in a DR scan and it matches the instruction. `ast_undefined_selects_bypass`: every opcode outside the defined set, and every defined opcode whose feature parameter is 0, selects the bypass register. `ast_zlb_bypasses_retimer`: under ZERO_LENGTH_BYPASS in Shift-DR, TDO follows TDI in the same step. `ast_idcode_caps_read_only`: the IDCODE and CAPS captures load the parameter constants and no Update-DR changes them. Covers: each of the 64 opcodes decoded, the bypass selection for one undefined opcode, a zero-length shift, a read of each capability TDR. |P1; the decode one-hot and the undefined-to-bypass rule P0
|T3 |`dtp_reset_props.sv` on the PTAP reset terms, `jtag_tmp`, `jtag_tmp_status_reg`, `jtag_ic_reset_reg`, `jtag_3dcr_reg` |`ast_trst_or_por_resets_tap`: either source low forces Test-Logic-Reset and the IDCODE instruction. `ast_tlr_asserts_rst_n`: `rst_n` is low exactly while the negedge-sampled state is Test-Logic-Reset. `ast_chrst_follows_rst_unless_persistent`: `chrst_n` equals `rst_n` while persistence is off and stays high in Test-Logic-Reset while it is on. `ast_tmp_on_only_by_clamp_hold`, `ast_tmp_off_by_release_or_escape`: the two TMP transitions and no other; the bypass escape needs Update-IR, an explicit BYPASS opcode and the escape bit. `ast_tmp_survives_tlr`: Test-Logic-Reset leaves the TMP state unchanged. `ast_ic_reset_hold_blocks_tlr`: with `reset_hold` 0, Test-Logic-Reset leaves `reset_enable` and `reset_control` unchanged; with `reset_hold` 1 it restores them to all-ones. `ast_ic_reset_trst_clears_all`: TRST restores every IC_RESET bit including `reset_hold`. `ast_ic_reset_ovrd_is_not_enable`: `.ovrd` is the inversion of `reset_enable` and `.val` equals `reset_control`. `ast_3dcr_config_hold_blocks_tlr`: the PTAP 3DCR survives Test-Logic-Reset while `config_hold` is set and clears on TRST. Covers: persistence on then off by each exit, an IC_RESET override held across a Test-Logic-Reset, a 3DCR held across a Test-Logic-Reset. |P0
|T4 |`dtp_stap_props.sv` on the PTAP 3DCR, each `jtag_stap`, and the `stap_host` terms of `jtag_intf_unit` |`ast_stap_disable_forces_sel_low`, `ast_stap_disable_tdo_low`, `ast_stap_disable_blocks_3dcr_update`: with the STAP's disable field high, `stap_sel` is 0, `host_tdo_o` is 0, and the 3DCR update register holds its value. `ast_stap_unselected_parks_tms`: with `stap_sel` 0, `host_tap_ctrl_o.tms` equals `tms_hold`; with 1 it follows the client TMS. `ast_stap_tdo_oen_only_when_selected_shifting`: `host_tdo_oen_o` is `stap_sel AND shift_en`. `ast_stap_host_disable_loops_back`: with `stap_host` disabled the PTAP scan input is the chain's own output and every host scan-control enable is 0. `ast_ptap_stap_select_routes_tdo`: with the PTAP 3DCR select set, TDO comes from the STAP chain, else from the TDR multiplexer. `ast_dbg_disable_sync_fail_closed`: each synchronizer resets to 1 and follows its input after two `tck` edges. Covers: the I/O, SMC, SEP and extra STAP each selected through the composed scan; a STAP disabled while selected; the return path disabled during a shift. |P0
|T5 |`dtp_sib_props.sv` on the three SIBs |`ast_sib_disable_forces_en_low`, `ast_sib_disable_blocks_update`, `ast_sib_disable_host_quiet`: with the SIB's field high, the segment enable is 0, the SIB bit does not update, and the host `capture_en`, `shift_en`, `update_en`, `select` and `host_scan_out_o` are 0. `ast_sib_open_routes_host`: with the enable set, the client scan output is the host scan input, else the SIB's own bit. `ast_sib_host_ctrl_follows_client`: the host enables equal the client enables AND `select` while enabled. `ast_sib_chain_order`: the DFD SIB's input is the non-secure SIB's output, whose input is the secure SIB's output. Covers: every SIB pattern (eight), each SIB disabled while open. |P0
|T6 |`dtp_dbgctl_props.sv` on `jtag_debug_ctrl_reg`; `ctn_clkstop_props.sv` on `ctn_clock_stop_ctrl` |`ast_dbgctl_rw_bits_hold`: bits 3:0 change only on an Update-DR of the DEBUG_CONTROL TDR. `ast_dbgctl_ro_bit_captures_status`: bit 4 captures the synchronized `cla_clock_stop` and ignores the shifted value. `ast_dbgctl_outputs_follow_update_reg`: the four outputs equal the update register bits. `ast_clkstop_or_after_three`: `stop_clks_o` equals the OR of `jtag_clock_stop_i` and every `clk_stop_req_i` bit sampled three `clk_i` cycles earlier. `ast_cla_status_excludes_jtag`: `cla_clock_stop_o` is the OR of the request bits alone, with no register. `ast_clkstop_low_in_reset`: `stop_clks_o` is 0 while `rst_ni` is low. Covers: a JTAG stop with no CLA request, a CLA request with no JTAG stop, the boot-stall override with each stall value. |P1
|T7 |`jtag2axi_ctrl_props.sv` on `jtag2axi`, one bind serving the three instances |`ast_j2a_state_valid`: the request machine holds one of its seven states. `ast_j2a_valid_only_in_send_states`: the TCK-side `aw_valid`, `w_valid` and `ar_valid` requests are high only in the address and data states. `ast_j2a_idle_after_status`: UPDATE_STATUS returns to IDLE in one cycle. `ast_j2a_status_encodes_resp`: OKAY, SLVERR and DECERR responses map to SUCCESS, SLVERR and DECERR, any other code to SLVERR. `ast_j2a_series_status_sticky`: a series error status holds until the series-control reset bit. `ast_j2a_admit_bounded_by_depth`: the in-flight and pushed counters never exceed the pipeline depth, itself clamped to `FIFO_DEPTH`. `ast_j2a_outstanding_saturates`: the ACLK outstanding counters never exceed their saturation value. `ast_j2a_disable_holds_idle`: with the disable high and the machine idle, it stays idle, no Update-DR latches, and the request FIFO and the buffered single request are cleared. `ast_j2a_disable_inflight_completes`: a disable that arrives outside IDLE does not abort the transaction in flight; the machine reaches IDLE through UPDATE_STATUS and then holds. `ast_j2a_release_replays_nothing`: after the disable falls, no request is issued until a new Update-DR. Covers: a single write and a single read to completion, a series of `FIFO_DEPTH` reads admitted, a SLVERR and a DECERR status, a disable landing in each non-idle state. |P0 for the disable and admit properties, P1 otherwise
|T8 |`ctn_xtrig_props.sv` on `ctp_handshake_ctrl`, `ctp_core`, `ctp_pulse_stretcher`, `ctm_src_selector` |`ast_hs_sender_states`, `ast_hs_receiver_states`: the two machines hold legal states and take only the four-phase arcs. `ast_hs_req_stable_until_ack`: `ct_req_out` holds until the synchronized ack. `ast_hs_ack_mirrors_req`: the receiver asserts ack on a synchronized request and drops it when the request drops. `ast_hs_dst_is_one_pulse`: a received request yields exactly one `ct_dst` cycle. `ast_hs_busy_lags_state`: `busy` is the registered not-idle of either machine. `ast_hs_reset_recovers_sender`: `reset_i` returns the sender to IDLE with `ct_req_out` low, and leaves the receiver untouched. `ast_ctp_mode_enables_exclusive`: the wire-OR and point-to-point pad enables are mutually exclusive and match `CONFIG.MODE`. `ast_ctp_wire_or_edge_pulse`: a rising synchronized request yields one stretcher trigger. `ast_stretch_width`: a trigger holds the output for `STRETCH_MULT + 1` cycles and a new trigger reloads the counter. `ast_ctm_slice_route`: `ct_src_o` equals the registered OR of `ct_dst AND select`, and an all-zero select isolates the output. `ast_ctp_status_reflects_pins`: `STATUS.REQ_IN`, `ACK_IN`, `ACK_OUT` equal the de-inverted pad values, and `STATUS.REQ_OUT` is 0 in wire-OR mode. Covers: a full four-phase cycle against the peer model, a stretched wire-OR pulse of the maximum assumed width, a route from an internal source to an external port and back, reset in each mode. |P1
|T9 |`ctn_csr_props.sv` on `cross_trigger_network` and one CTP and one CTM register block |`ast_csr_unmapped_decerr`: an address outside `0x000` to `0x2FF` completes with DECERR on both channels. `ast_csr_ctp_window`: an address in `0x200 + 16n` to `0x20F + 16n` reaches CTP `n` alone. `ast_csr_ctm_aliases`: within the CTM window only address bits 7:2 select a register, so `0x100` to `0x1FF` reads the same registers as `0x000` to `0x0FF`. `ast_csr_reset_values`: CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT and every CT_DST_SELECT read 0 after reset. `ast_csr_status_write_ignored`: a write to STATUS returns OKAY and changes nothing. `ast_csr_strobe_masks_bytes`: a write changes only the strobed bytes. `ast_csr_read_after_write`: a read after a completed write returns the written field value. Covers: a DECERR write and read, a CTP and a CTM read-after-write, a masked write. |P1
|===

[[dtp-formal-observations]]
==== Observations the properties settle

Reading the RTL against the design chapters leaves six points on which the
two differ or the chapter is silent; each is stated as a property above so
the proof records which holds.

* The JTAG integration chapter says a disabled bridge holds its AXI machine
  idle so that no traffic is generated; the RTL gates only the IDLE arm and
  flushes the buffers once IDLE is reached, so a transaction already in
  flight completes (`ast_j2a_disable_inflight_completes`).
* The cross-trigger network chapter describes `stop_clks_o` as registered;
  the RTL passes the OR through a two-flop synchronizer and an output flop,
  three `clk_i` cycles (`ast_clkstop_or_after_three`).
* The cross-trigger network chapter's internal-port remapping text reads
  `INT_CT_MODE[i] = 0` as point-to-point; the RTL's
  `MODE_WIRE_OR = ~INT_CT_MODE[i]` and the DTP parameter comment make 0 the
  wire-OR pulse mode (`ast_ctp_mode_enables_exclusive`, checked per internal
  port).
* `STATUS.REQ_OUT` reads 0 in wire-OR mode because the pad data is static
  and the pull is on the enable (`ast_ctp_status_reflects_pins`).
* `CONFIG.RESET` returns only the sender machine to IDLE; the receiver has no
  reset arc (`ast_hs_reset_recovers_sender`).
* The CTM is mapped over 512 bytes but decodes address bits 7:2 only, so the
  upper half of its window aliases the lower (`ast_csr_ctm_aliases`).

Two further points are reset X-safety questions for the licensed
X-propagation app: `cla_clock_stop_o` is combinational with no reset, and
the `cla_clock_stop` synchronizer inside `jtag_debug_ctrl_reg` has its reset
tied high.

[[dtp-formal-assumptions]]
==== Assumptions and environments

Every top has one environment module bound at the top, in the shape of
`formal/fpv/sby/dtp_sby_env.sv`: the asynchronous resets are free until the
first sampling edge and released afterwards, and the scan reset is inactive.
Beyond that:

* `dtp`: `asm_initial_reset` (the macro layer) pins the reset low in the first
  cycle; `asm_env_resets_released` and `asm_env_scan_reset_inactive` are the
  environment. `dbg_disable_i`, `tms`, `tdi` and every host scan input are
  free. The AXI responder ports are free too: the JTAG-domain targets never
  reach them.
* `jtag2axi`: the JTAG-side controls are free inputs constrained to the TAP's
  shape, `asm_j2a_scan_ctrl_exclusive` (capture, shift and update never
  overlap) and `asm_j2a_update_follows_shift_or_capture`. The AXI responder
  follows `asm_j2a_resp_only_when_outstanding` (a B or R beat arrives only
  with a request outstanding) and `asm_j2a_ready_valid_stable` (a valid holds
  until its ready); these are the handshake rules the shared AXI checker
  states as assertions, written as booleans in the environment module.
* `cross_trigger_network`: the AXI-Lite manager follows the same two
  handshake assumptions; `asm_ctp_peer_four_phase` models the far end of a
  point-to-point port (ack rises after a synchronized request and falls
  after it drops); `asm_ctp_stretch_small` bounds `STRETCH_MULT` to 4 in the
  end-to-end tasks. The pad inputs are otherwise free.

Every `cov_` label stays reachable under these assumptions; an unreached
cover fails the item, and an assumption change re-runs every cover of its
top (`formal-property-style.adoc`, cover reachability).

[[dtp-formal-depths]]
==== Tasks and depths

Each block has its own `bmc`, `cover` and `prove` task triple. On the `dtp`
top the triples share `formal/fpv/sby/dtp.sby`, selected by task tags, and
one app table per block passes its triple in `args`, so `testlists/formal.toml`
lists one item per block. The `jtag2axi` and `cross_trigger_network` tops have
their own task files. The `prove` task carries no depth; `bmc` and `cover`
share one depth per block, set above the step at which the block's deepest
cover is reachable.

The bound follows the block's structural latency. Under `multiclock on` every
step is a time point and one `tck` cycle costs two steps; the reset
environment releases the resets at the first `tck` posedge, so a state that is
`n` `tck` edges from Test-Logic-Reset is first observable at step `3 + 2n`.
`clk_i` is a second explicit clock on the `dtp` top and toggles freely, so a
`clk_i`-domain latency of `k` cycles adds steps only where `2k` exceeds the
`tck` path. The `cross_trigger_network` top is single-clock and its depth is
in `clk_i` cycles. Two `tck` edge counts recur: an instruction load from
Test-Logic-Reset is 13 edges (Run-Test/Idle, Select-DR, Select-IR,
Capture-IR, Shift-IR, six shifts with the last leaving to Exit1-IR,
Update-IR), and a DR scan of `b` bits is `b + 4` edges (Select-DR,
Capture-DR, Shift-DR, `b` shifts with the last leaving to Exit1-DR,
Update-DR).

[cols="1,4,1,1,1",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Deepest cover and its distance |Edges or cycles |Step |Depth
|T1 |`cov_tap_tlr_from_five_tms_high`: Run-Test/Idle, Select-DR, Capture-DR, then five TMS-high edges into Test-Logic-Reset |8 |19 |30
|T2 |An undefined opcode loaded (13), then a DR scan captures the bypass 0 and shifts it to TDO (Select-DR, Capture-DR, Shift-DR, one shift) |17 |37 |40
|T3 |CLAMP_HOLD loaded (13), persistence on at the next edge (14), five TMS-high edges into Test-Logic-Reset with `chrst_n` held high (19), BYPASS loaded from there (32), persistence off at the edge after Update-IR |33 |69 |75; an IC_RESET override held across Test-Logic-Reset needs 29 edges, step 61
|T4 |TAP_3DCR loaded (13); a 2-bit DR scan sets the PTAP select (19); a 6-bit composed scan through the two PTAP bits and four SIB bits opens the SMC STAP's SIB (29); a 9-bit composed scan sets its `stap_sel` (42); the parked TMS releases at the next edge |43 |89 |100; the long pole of the plan, driven by three scans through the chain
|T5 |SELECT_IJTAG loaded (13); a 3-bit DR scan opens one SIB (20); the next Capture-DR shows the host select (22); one shift through the opened segment (24) |24 |51 |55
|T6 |DEBUG_CONTROL loaded (13); a 5-bit DR scan sets `jtag_clock_stop` (22); `stop_clks_o` follows three `clk_i` cycles later, within the next `tck` cycle; the next Capture-DR reads back `cla_clock_stop` (24) |24 |51 |55 on `dtp`; the `ctn_clkstop_fpv` triple on the single-clock top uses depth 10
|T7 |An Update-DR requests a single write (1); the machine leaves IDLE two edges later (3); the CDC delivers `aw_valid` after four `clk_i` cycles; the responder answers within four cycles; the response crosses back in four `tck` edges and the status is capturable at the next edge (about 12 `tck` edges with the `clk_i` path inside them) |12 |27 |30, with the two clocks free; a series of `FIFO_DEPTH` reads adds one admit per edge
|T8 |A CSR write sets point-to-point mode (about 6 cycles), a source pulse starts the sender (2), the peer model acks after the two-flop synchronizer (4), the sender drops the request and returns to IDLE after the ack drops (6); the wire-OR route from an internal source to a pad enable is 6 cycles and a stretched pulse of the assumed maximum width adds 5 |20 |20 |30
|T9 |An AXI-Lite write through the crossbar and the register block takes effect (about 6 cycles), a read of the same field returns it (about 6 more); a DECERR completion is within 4 |14 |14 |20
|===

A cover added at a greater distance raises its block's depth. The T1 depth
also follows a diameter-plus-reset argument: depth 30 is thirteen `tck` edges
after reset release, the state-graph diameter of seven plus a five-edge TMS
reset sequence with one edge of margin.

[[dtp-formal-apps]]
==== App checks and path assignment

[cols="2,2,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Check |Path |Targets |What it establishes
|Property checking (`fpv`) |Open, `sby` |T1 to T9 as listed |The properties above, with `prove` for the P0 set and bounded `bmc` plus `cover` at the block depth.
|Connectivity |Licensed connectivity app, companion-owned config |T4, T5 and the static wiring of `dtp`: the STAP chain order I/O, SMC, SEP, extra, return; each `dbg_disable_i` field to exactly its path (the eleven fields of the simulation bench's 22-cell contract); the IC_RESET slice packing SMC, SEP, EXT to `reset_hold`; the pad-ring tie-offs on the unused CTP directions |Point-to-point wiring the property style cannot express as a single boolean without naming every net; on the open path the tie-offs and the chain order are constant assertions inside T4 and T5.
|CSR access policy from the register description |Licensed register app driven by the SystemRDL under `hw/ip/cross_trigger/*/regs/` |T9 |Every field's access, reset value and side effect for the sixteen CTPs and the CTM; the open path carries the address map and one slice.
|Security-path checks for the debug-disable gating |Open, `sby`, the P0 set of T4, T5 and T7 |T4, T5, T7 |With a disable field high, its path's select, TDO, host enables and bridge machine are quiet, and a release replays nothing; unbounded by `prove`.
|Reset X-safety |Licensed X-propagation app |T6, T7 |The two unreset paths named in <<dtp-formal-observations>>, and the JTAG2AXI CDC after an ACLK reset mid-transaction.
|Unreachability |Licensed unreachability app over the merged simulation coverage database |F4.4 hole entries |Whether the reset-timing and CDC clear/isolate bins and the long-wait poll bins in `cov/config/verilator/coverage_policy.toml` are unreachable by construction, which turns their hole entries into proven dispositions.
|===

The licensed apps run through the same runner from the companion's formal
configuration, named by the site layer (`run-dv.adoc`, Formal verification);
their scripts read the same property files. The open tree carries no licensed
collateral.

[[dtp-formal-running]]
==== Running and grading

[source,bash]
----
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal                    # the smoke group: dtp_tap_fpv
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --items fpv        # every formal item
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --items dtp_stap_fpv
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --dry-run          # the rendered sby command
----

The `flist` stage regenerates `formal/build/dtp.f` from Bender; the `formal`
stage runs one item's `bmc`, `cover` and `prove` tasks in one `sby`
invocation whose work directories are `<run>/stages/formal/<item>_<task>/`.
The `smoke` group holds `dtp_tap_fpv`; the `fpv` group holds every item. The
runner grades an item from the task status files and JUnit reports, never
from the exit code: every task `PASS` with every cover reached is `PASS`; a
counterexample, an induction failure or an unreached cover is `FAIL` in the
`formal_fail` bucket, and `result.json` carries the `formal` object with the
proven and covered counts (`run-dv.adoc`, Formal result grading).

The FAIL path is checked by inverting one assertion body, for example
`dr_select_i == ir_select_i` in `ast_tap_select_exclusive`, and running the
same command: the `bmc` task reports `FAIL` with the counterexample trace at
`stages/formal/dtp_tap_fpv_bmc/engine_0/trace.vcd`, the `prove` task reports
`FAIL`, and the item is `FAIL`.

The open path needs a yosys-slang build with the concurrent-assertion
lowering and an SMT solver beside `sby` (`formal-property-style.adoc`,
Open-path toolchain); the runner guide's Formal verification chapter covers
the local and container runs and the licensed backends. Formal mode is a
developer-host flow: hosted CI runs no formal job.

[[dtp-formal-dependencies]]
==== Dependencies between targets

T3 and T4 hold the P0 set and stand alone on the `dtp` top. T7 and T8 share
the boolean handshake assumption set for the AXI and AXI-Lite responders and
the point-to-point peer model, so that environment precedes both. The
licensed connectivity, register, X-propagation and unreachability apps read
the same property files from the companion's formal configuration. A target
is complete when its item passes with every cover reached and its FAIL path
is checked.

=== Regression Groups

The simulation groups below come from `testlists/all.toml`;
`testlists/formal.toml` carries the formal item `dtp_tap_fpv` in its own
`smoke` group (<<dtp-formal-plan>>).

[cols=",,",options="header",]
|===
|Group |Tests |Purpose
|`smoke` |`dtp_sanity_test`, `dtp_jtag_idcode_test` |Fast TAP bring-up gate
|`functional` |Smoke plus the JTAG2AXI `single_write` and `single_write_read` scenarios |Fast functional regression
|`basic_jtag` |The Smoke and Basic JTAG scenarios |TAP, instruction, reset, BSR-loopback, and TMP smoke coverage
|`debug_tdr` |The TMP_STATUS, IC_RESET, DEBUG_CONTROL, and CAPS tests |Debug TDR coverage
|`all` |Every scenario-expanded test (`expected_count`-pinned in `testlists/all.toml`) |Full OCAH DTP regression
|`jtag2axi` |All JTAG2AXI write/read/error/robustness scenarios |AXI/AXI-Lite bridge coverage
|`scan` |STAP, 3DCR, and iJTAG scenarios |IEEE 1838 and IEEE 1687 coverage
|`dbg_disable` |The two debug-disable matrix tests plus every resource-specific directed gating test |Per-field `dbg_disable_t` closure (22-cell coverage contract)
|`xtrig` |XTRIG, CTP, and CTM scenarios |Cross-trigger and CSR coverage
|===

[[dtp-vplan-signoff]]
=== Component Signoff

One subsection per plan section. Each records the evidence contract its
scenarios meet in both realizations, the negative-validation hooks that must
fail a run, the disposition of every checker-table row whose realization
differs from its plan text, and the functional-coverage points the group
fires. Feature credit stays with `DTP_SCOPE_TRACEABILITY.adoc`; the group
sizes are the `expected_count` guards in `testlists/all.toml`.

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-jtag]]
==== Primary JTAG and boundary scan (section 0)

The section 0 scenarios are the `basic_jtag` group and realize the F1
rows of the scope matrix: the IEEE 1149.1 state machine and its resets,
instruction decode and the BYPASS family, IDCODE, the boundary-scan
instructions against the bench's compact loopback, RUNBIST, and the two
test-mode-persistence clamp instructions. `smoke` carries `dtp_sanity_test`
and `dtp_jtag_idcode_test` as the PR gate; `all` carries the group into the
nightly and weekly regressions. The formal item `dtp_tap_fpv`
(`testlists/formal.toml`, target T1) proves the TAP controller's one-hot
state, the five-TMS reset, and the legal transitions where SymbiYosys is
installed (`run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal`); it corroborates F1.1 and
F1.2 and is not regression credit.

Every scenario runs at least sixteen seeded passes. A pass records named
`CHK-*` evidence through the shared JTAG checker and finalizes it once; a
pass with zero checks or a missing required ID fails. The passive pin-level
scan monitor cross-checks every IR and DR scan the sequence issues
(`CHK-SCAN-COUNT`, `CHK-SCAN-IR-LEN`, `CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN`), and `CHK-NONVAC`
records that the pass compared something. On SV-UVM the always-on
`dtp_scoreboard` features `ir_decode`, `idcode`, and `bypass` end each run
as `CHK-SB-IR-DECODE`, `CHK-SB-IDCODE`, and `CHK-SB-BYPASS`, and the
`dtp_tap_fsm_checker` ends it as `CHK-TAP-STATE`. Each procedure's
*Evidence* field names the records it lands.

[cols="1,2,3,2,1",options="header",]
|===
|Row |Tests |Named evidence |Coverage points |Credit
|F1.1 TAP state machine |`dtp_sanity_test` |SV-UVM: `CHK-TAP-STATE`, `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR`, `CHK-TAP-TLR-TMS5`, `CHK-TAP-GOTO` required; `bypass` and `idcode` scoreboard features. cocotb: the state-visit and transition checks are plain assertions of the sequence (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-jtag-dispositions>>) |`c_state_*` (the sixteen states), `c_arc_*` (the thirty-two legal arcs) |YES
|F1.2 TAP reset |`dtp_jtag_trst_test`, `dtp_jtag_trst_por_independence_test`, `dtp_jtag_tlr_reset_test` |`CHK-TAP-TRST-TLR`, `CHK-TAP-TLR-TMS5`, `CHK-TAP-TLR-IDCODE`, `CHK-IDCODE-RAW`, `CHK-IDCODE-RECOVERY`, `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR`, `CHK-TAP-STATE` |`c_smoke_reset_source_trst`, `c_smoke_reset_source_tms_to_tlr`, `c_reset_trst_from_active`, `c_reset_tms5_from_active` |YES
|F1.3 IDCODE |`dtp_jtag_idcode_test` |`CHK-IDCODE-MARKER`, `CHK-IDCODE-MANUFACTURER`, `CHK-IDCODE-PART-NUMBER`, `CHK-IDCODE-VERSION`, `CHK-IDCODE-RAW`, `CHK-IDCODE-STABLE`; `idcode` scoreboard feature |`c_smoke_idcode_default_after_reset`, `c_smoke_idcode_explicit_instruction`, `c_smoke_idcode_marker_1` |YES
|F1.4 Instruction decode and BYPASS |`dtp_jtag_bypass_test`, `dtp_jtag_inv_bypass_test`, `dtp_jtag_zero_length_bypass_test`, `dtp_jtag_undef_instr_test` |`CHK-BYPASS-00`, `CHK-BYPASS-3F`, `CHK-BYPASS-LATENCY`, `CHK-BYPASS-DELAY`, `CHK-INV-BYPASS`, `CHK-ZLB-PASSTHROUGH`, `CHK-IR-DECODE`, `CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN` (the one-bit and zero-bit chain lengths); `ir_decode` and `bypass` scoreboard features. The undefined-instruction scenario records through the `bypass` feature on SV-UVM and through plain assertions on cocotb (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-jtag-dispositions>>) |`c_ir_opcode_*` (all sixty-four encodings), `c_ir_category_*`, `c_ir_behavior_enabled_instruction`, `c_ir_behavior_undefined_to_bypass` |YES
|F1.5 Boundary-scan instructions |`dtp_jtag_sample_preload_test`, `dtp_jtag_extest_test`, `dtp_jtag_intest_test`, `dtp_jtag_clamp_test`, `dtp_jtag_highz_test`, `dtp_jtag_ac_extest_train_test`, `dtp_jtag_ac_extest_pulse_test` |`CHK-BSR-LOOPBACK`, `CHK-IR-DECODE`, `CHK-BYPASS-DELAY` (the CLAMP and HIGHZ bypass path), `CHK-BSR-SELECT` (SV-UVM), the scan cross-check IDs |The seven instructions' `c_ir_opcode_*` points; `cg_scan_boundary` on the commercial simulators |YES against the compact identity loopback (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-jtag-dispositions>>)
|F1.6 RUNBIST and the TMP clamps |`dtp_jtag_runbist_test`, `dtp_jtag_clamp_hold_test`, `dtp_jtag_clamp_release_test` |`CHK-RUNBIST-RESPONSE`, `CHK-TMP-PERSIST`, `CHK-IR-DECODE`, `CHK-BSR-LOOPBACK` |The three instructions' `c_ir_opcode_*` points; `c_tmp_persistence_*` in `dtp_debug_tdr_fcov` |YES
|===

The instruction encodings, TAP-state encodings, and IDCODE fields the checks
compare against are transcriptions of the `jtag_intf_unit` and `jtag_ptap`
documentation, each table naming the document and section it copies
(`cocotb/env/dtp_types.py`). The responder memory and the design collateral
are the only other sources of expected values; nothing is derived from the
observation under check.

Two hooks prove that the evidence path gates the verdict. Each is an
environment variable on cocotb and a plusarg of the same name on SV-UVM, and
a run with either set must fail:

* `DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE` corrupts every family expectation
  (loopback, bypass delay, decoded instruction, TMP persistence) in every
  scenario that attaches the family checker.
* `DTP_JTAG_TAP_CHECKER_NEGATIVE` arms a wrong IDCODE and a wrong TAP-state
  prediction in the reset-family scenarios and, on SV-UVM, in the sanity
  scenario's TAP checker.

[source,bash]
----
DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE=1 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_jtag_extest_test
DTP_JTAG_TAP_CHECKER_NEGATIVE=1 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_jtag_tlr_reset_test
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_sanity_test --plusarg +DTP_JTAG_TAP_CHECKER_NEGATIVE
----

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-jtag-dispositions]]
===== Plan-to-realization dispositions

Each row is a section 0 checker whose realization differs from its plan
text, with where the behavior is proven and the scope of any waiver.

[cols="2,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Realization |Disposition
|`sanity_fsm_visit_chk`, `sanity_bypass_latency_chk`, `sanity_scan_path_chk` |cocotb: plain assertions of the sequence with no attached checker, so `DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE` does not reach them and any mismatch fails the pass directly. SV-UVM: `CHK-TAP-STATE`, `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR`, `CHK-TAP-TLR-TMS5`, `CHK-TAP-GOTO`, `CHK-BYPASS-LATENCY`, and the `CHK-IDCODE-*` records through the env checker |Named evidence on the SV-UVM twin; the cocotb sequence records no named evidence
|`undef_bypass_fallback_chk`, `undef_no_tdr_select_chk`, `undef_no_error_chk` |cocotb: the bypass-delay and pattern checks run as plain assertions. SV-UVM: the `bypass` scoreboard feature (`CHK-SB-BYPASS`) and checks tagged with the family IDs |Named evidence on the SV-UVM twin; the cocotb sequence records no named evidence
|`runbist_instr_ungated_chk`, `dft_sib_gated_chk`, `dft_sib_restored_chk` |`dtp_jtag_runbist_test` proves acceptance (`CHK-IR-DECODE`) and a live response (`CHK-RUNBIST-RESPONSE`) and drives no disable; the `dft_nonsecure` gate is proven by `dtp_ijtag_dft_test` and the scan matrix |Credited under F3.2 and F3.6; the three rows are plan text without a realization in this test
|`tlr_tdr_default_chk` |`dtp_jtag_tlr_reset_test` proves the IDCODE reload and the return to Run-Test/Idle; the IC_RESET hold behavior across Test-Logic-Reset is `dtp_jtag_ic_reset_test`, the DEBUG_CONTROL reset value is the debug-control scenarios, the 3DCR `config_hold` behavior is `dtp_3dcr_config_hold_test` |Credited under F2.2, F2.3, F2.4, and F3.5
|`extest_output_drive_chk`, `extest_scan_ctrl_chk`, `intest_input_drive_chk`, `intest_scan_ctrl_chk`, `clamp_output_hold_chk`, `clamp_glitch_free_chk`, `highz_oen_deassert_chk`, `highz_recover_chk`, `preload_no_drive_chk`, `extest_train_pattern_chk`, `extest_train_timing_chk`, `extest_train_no_disturb_chk`, `extest_pulse_single_chk`, `extest_pulse_timing_chk` |The bench models no boundary cells: the BSR is the compact identity loopback of `DtpScanModel` (TB_ARCH, DUT-local model ownership). The scenarios prove instruction decode, chain length, and capture and shift through that loopback (`CHK-BSR-LOOPBACK`, `CHK-BSR-SELECT`) and the bypass path of CLAMP and HIGHZ; pin drive, output enables, and glitch-free transitions have no observable on this bench |Scoped waiver: boundary-cell pin behavior is outside this bench. The waiver covers exactly these fourteen rows and applies only while the bench has no boundary-cell model with pin observables
|`inv_bypass_no_side_effect_chk` |No direct check; the `ir_decode` scoreboard feature and the bypass reference model bound what INV_BYPASS selects |Credited through the scoreboard features
|===

===== Coverage review

The `basic_jtag` group under Verilator fires every `c_state_*` point, every
`c_arc_*` point, both `c_reset_*` reachability points, all five `c_smoke_*`
points, and the `c_ir_behavior_enabled_instruction` and
`c_ir_behavior_undefined_to_bypass` points of `dtp_fcov`. Of the sixty-four
`c_ir_opcode_*` points it fires the IEEE 1149.1 and IEEE 1838 instructions,
RUNBIST, the two clamp instructions, TMP_STATUS, and every reserved and
undefined encoding. The encodings it leaves unfired are IC_RESET, TAP_3DCR,
DEBUG_CONTROL, JTAG_CAPS, SELECT_IJTAG, and the JTAG2AXI capability and
transfer TDRs, together with `c_ir_category_ijtag`,
`c_ir_category_jtag2axi`, and `c_ir_behavior_disabled_to_bypass`; those are
the instructions of F2 through F5 and fire in the `debug_tdr`, `scan`,
`jtag2axi`, and `dbg_disable` groups. On the commercial simulators the
`cg_tap_state`, `cg_tap_arc`, and `cg_jtag_instruction` covergroups carry
the same bins and `cg_scan_boundary` adds the loopback observations.

=== Known Limitations

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Limitation |Impact
|iJTAG scan chains are looped back (`scan_in <- scan_out`) in `tb_top` |iJTAG scenarios validate the DTP-side SIB plumbing, not an external instrument network
|The downstream STAP TAPs are behavioral `ocah_jtag_vip` devices (5-bit IR, IDCODE, one writable TDR) attached only by the STAP-selection scenarios |Other scan scenarios see the STAP host ports as wire loopbacks
|The bring-up startup vector clears all debug disables |Gating behavior is only observable under explicit disable stimulus; the `dbg_disable` group drives directed and seeded disable vectors and closes the 22-cell coverage contract
|SV covergroups are not supported by Verilator |Functional coverage is collected by the `cov/sv/` cover-property modules under Verilator and by covergroups on commercial simulators (see `DTP_FCOV.adoc`)
|===
